Trito-non-ordinary Iwasawa theory of diagonal cycles
Raúl Alonso, Kâzım Büyükboduk, Antonio Cauchi, Antonio Lei

Abstract
. Our goal in this paper is to introduce and study the Euler system of signed diagonal cycles associated with a trito-non-ordinary triple product of the form f B × g B × h B f^{B}\times g^{B}\times\textup{\bf h}^{B} , where f B f^{B} (resp. g B g^{B} ) is a p p -ordinary (resp. non-ordinary) eigenform on an indefinite quaternion algebra B / ℚ B_{/\mathbb{Q}} of weight 2 2 , and h B \textup{\bf h}^{B} is a primitive Hida ( p p -ordinary) family. When B = M 2 ​ ( ℚ ) B=\mathrm{M}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}) is split and h = h B \textup{\bf h}=\textup{\bf h}^{B} has CM by an imaginary quadratic field, this allows us to develop the signed anticyclotomic Iwasawa theory for the base change BC K / ℚ ​ ( π f ) × BC K / ℚ ​ ( π g ) × ψ {\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{f})\times{\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{g})\times\psi , where ψ \psi is a Hecke character of K K . We formulate a signed Perrin-Riou-style Iwasawa main conjecture in this setting, and obtain a result on one inclusion in this conjecture. Our methods also allow us to extend Hsieh’s construction of the balanced triple-product p p -adic L L -function to the trito-non-ordinary scenario, and to define its signed counterparts.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11F66, 11F67, 11F33; Secondary 11F85, 11G18, 14F30

2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
11R23 (primary)

1.
Introduction

Throughout this article,
p
is a fixed odd prime number. Once and for all, we fix embeddings
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
and
\iota_{p}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

The construction of Gross–Kudla–Schoen diagonal cycles in the product of three Kuga–Sato varieties was initially studied in
[
23
,
24
]
. Darmon and Rotger later incorporated these cycles into the Euler system framework and demonstrated that they satisfy a
p
-adic Gross–Zagier formula via the
p
-adic Abel–Jacobi map and established a connection of these cycles with the Garrett–Rankin
p
-adic
L
-function
[
16
,
17
]
. This development has ignited extensive research into diagonal cycles and their applications in Iwasawa theory. Important progress was made in
[
3
]
, where the authors established the variational properties of these cycles in families of modular forms, and proved explicit reciprocity laws linking diagonal cycles to three-variable
p
-adic
L
-functions.

In a different direction, the construction of families of diagonal cycles has been adapted to define anticyclotomic Euler systems for the product of two elliptic modular forms in
[
1
,
2
]
, building on the framework developed in
[
29
]
. These works focus exclusively on the case where all three factors involved in the (families of) triple products are
p
-ordinary.

In the present article, we construct unbounded (resp. bounded signed) anticyclotomic Euler systems of diagonal cycles for the twisted base change
{\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{f})\times{\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{g})\times\psi
, where
f
(resp.
g
) is a
p
-ordinary (resp.
p
-non-ordinary) eigenform,
K
is an imaginary quadratic field, and
\psi
is a Hecke character of
K
(see Theorem
B
and Theorem
C
below). Along the way, we also construct unbounded (resp. bounded signed) balanced triple-product
p
-adic
L
-functions (in a single variable; see Theorem
E
below), extending the work of Hsieh
[
28
]
to the trito-non-ordinary
1
1

1

From the Greek prefix
trito-
, meaning “one-third”.
setting, which we believe is of independent interest.

The main arithmetic applications of these constructions are towards (signed) Iwasawa main conjectures for
{\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{f})\times{\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{g})\times\psi
, which we formulate in §
1.2.5
. Employing the Euler system machinery of Jetchev–Nekovář–Skinner
[
29
]
, we prove a containment in this conjecture (cf. Theorem
D
). We remark that, assuming that the Abel–Jacobi map
{\rm cl}_{1}
is injective, the non-triviality of this containment follows from
[
45
]
(proof of the Gross–Kudla conjecture), provided that we have
L^{\prime}({\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{f})\times{\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{g})\times\psi,2)\neq 0
for some Hecke character
\psi
of
K
whose theta-series is a weight-2 specialisation of the CM Hida family
h
relevant to our discussion (see §
3.1
).

To explain our main results, we first introduce some notation.

1.1.
Diagonal cycles for towers of threefolds

Let
N=N^{+}N^{-}
be a positive integer coprime to
p
, where
N^{+}
and
N^{-}
are coprime and
N^{-}
is square-free. Let
B
be the indefinite quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
of discriminant
N^{-}
. We also put
E=\mathbb{Q}^{\oplus 3}
and
B_{E}=B^{\oplus 3}
. Once and for all fix an isomorphism

\Psi=\prod_{q\nmid N^{-}}\Psi_{q}\,:\quad B(\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{(N^{-})})\simeq M_{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{(N^{-})}).

We fix a maximal order
\mathcal{O}_{B}
such that
\Psi_{q}(\mathcal{O}_{B}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q})=M_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{q})
for all
q\nmid\infty N^{-}
. We then let
U_{0}(N)
be the open compact subgroup of
\widehat{B}^{\times}
defined by the Eichler order
R_{N}
of level
N^{+}
in
\mathcal{O}_{B}
and let

U_{1}(N)=\{g\in U_{0}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{q}(g_{q})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\star&\star\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }N\mathbb{Z}_{q})\text{ for }q|N^{+}\}.

For a natural number
n
, we put

\displaystyle U_{n}

\displaystyle:=\{g\in U_{1}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\star\\
&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\,,\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}\,,

\displaystyle U_{1,n}

\displaystyle:=\{g\in U_{1}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\star&\star\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\}\,.

We write
S_{n}
for the Shimura curve given by
U_{n}
.
Let us denote by
\iota:B\hookrightarrow B_{E}
the diagonal embedding, and let
\mathcal{U}_{1}(N):=U_{1}(N)^{\times 3}\subseteq\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}
. For each
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{3}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{g\in\mathcal{U}_{1}(N)\,:\,g\equiv\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\star\\
0&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{1}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\star\\
0&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{2}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\star\\
0&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{3}}\right)\,,\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right\}.

The corresponding Shimura threefold is denoted by
\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
. Via a twisted diagonal embedding of the Shimura curve
S_{n}
where
n=\max\{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\}
, we define the diagonal cycle
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\in{\rm CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}})
(see Definition
2.6
). The general machinery of Loeffler given in
[
36
]
implies that these classes satisfy a norm relation given by the Hecke operator:

{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n+1}}})=\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}.

1.2.
Main results

Let
f
and
g
be weight-two forms on
B
of level
U_{1}(N)
. We assume that
f
is
p
-ordinary and
g
is
p
-non-ordinary.

1.2.1.

Our primary goal in the present paper is to build an anticyclotomic Euler system for the Rankin–Selberg product of
f
and
g
using the classes
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
. In particular, we show that there exist signed integral classes, in the spirit of the constructions in
[
10
,
7
,
11
,
8
,
43
,
12
]
. To do so, we first prove that the classes naturally defined by
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
satisfy a three-term norm relation. More precisely, starting off with
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
, we define a class in Galois cohomology

\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2)))
, where
\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}
denotes the Shimura threefold of level
U_{n_{1}}\times U_{n_{2}}\times U_{n_{3}}
, which in turn gives rise to

\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g),\ \widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{f}^{*}\otimes T_{g}^{*}\otimes e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}))\,,

where
T_{f}^{*}
and
T_{g}^{*}
are
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-representations attached to
f
and
g
, realized as quotients of
H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{1})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{0})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, respectively.
Our first main result is the following norm relations of these classes.

Theorem A

(Corollary
2.16
)
.

For any positive integer
n
, we have

\displaystyle({\rm id},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\Delta_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)

\displaystyle=a_{p}(g)\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)-\chi_{g}(p)\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)\,,

\displaystyle({\rm id},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)

\displaystyle=p\cdot\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)\,,

where
\pi_{1}
denotes the natural projection map
X(U_{n+1})\to X(U_{n})
.

1.2.2.

We assume until §
1.2.6
that
B=\mathrm{M}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. We fix
K
to be an imaginary quadratic field where
p
splits. We write
(p)=\mathfrak{p}\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
, where
\mathfrak{p}
is the prime of
K
determined by our fixed embedding
\iota_{p}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. We assume that
p
does not divide the class number of
K
. Let
\Lambda=\mathcal{O}\llbracket 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rrbracket
, where
\mathcal{O}
is the ring of integers of a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
that contains all the coefficients of
f
and
g
. Let
\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}
be the Galois group of the anticyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
. We can identify
\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}
with the anti-diagonal in
(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cong\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathfrak{p}}^{(1)}\times\mathcal{O}_{K,\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}^{(1)}
via the Artin map. Let

\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}\xrightarrow{\,\kappa_{\textup{ac}}\,}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
be the character defined by mapping the element
((1+p)^{-1},(1+p))
to
1+p
, and
\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}\xrightarrow{\,\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}\,}\Lambda^{\times}
the character defined by mapping the element
((1+p)^{-1},(1+p))
to the group-like element
[1+p]
. For an integer
n\geq 1
, let
\Gamma_{n}=(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{p^{n-1}}
and let
\Lambda_{n}=\mathcal{O}[\Gamma_{n}]
.

Let
\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}
be the
p
-adic avatar of a Hecke character
\varphi_{0}
of
K
of conductor dividing
\mathfrak{cp}
and infinity type
(-1,0)
, where
\mathfrak{c}
is coprime to
p
and
\mathfrak{P}
is the maximal ideal of a sufficiently large extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(cf. §
3.1.1
). Let
\chi_{\varphi_{0}}
denote the central character of
\varphi_{0}
. We impose the following self-duality condition.

(
SD
)

\chi_{f}\chi_{g}\chi_{\varphi_{0}}\varepsilon_{K}=\omega^{2a}
for some
a\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
. Let
K[m]
denote the maximal
p
-subextension of the ring class field of
K
of conductor
m
.
Through suitable modifications of the classes
{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)
, we build on the ideas developed in
[
32
]
to define classes

z_{n,m},\,\tilde{z}_{n,m}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{f}^{*}\otimes T_{g}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{K[m]}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Lambda_{n}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})(2)),

where
c
denotes the complex conjugation. By Shapiro’s isomorphism, these classes can also be regarded as classes in

H^{1}(K[m],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})(2))\simeq H^{1}(K[mp^{n}],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2))\,.

1.2.3.

Let us put
M=T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2)
to ease our notation, and consider the following condition.

(
H
0
)

The residual
G_{K}
-representation
\overline{M}
satisfies
H^{0}(K,\overline{M})=0
.

Building on Theorem
A
, we prove the following norm relations for the classes
z_{n,m}
.

Theorem B

(Propositions
3.7
and
3.12
)
.

Suppose that (
(H0)
) holds. Let
n
be a positive integer,
m
a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
that are split in
K
, and
q
a prime coprime to
Nmp
that is split in
K
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{cor}_{K[mqp^{n}]/K[mp^{n}]}(z_{n,mq})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{n,m},

where
\mathcal{P}_{q}
is an explicit Euler factor.
Furthermore, if
n\geq 2
, then

\mathrm{cor}_{K[mp^{n+1}]/K[mp^{n}]}(z_{n+1,m})=a_{p}(g)z_{n,m}-\chi_{g}(p)\mathrm{res}_{K[mp^{n-1}]/K[mp^{n}]}(z_{n-1,m}),

where
\chi_{g}
is the nebentype of
g
.

1.2.4.

The second norm relation in Theorem
B
is similar to the one satisfied by Heegner points on an elliptic curve. It is precisely this relation that allows us to apply the machinery of Sprung to define signed integral classes. For
n\geq 1
, let
C_{g,n}
denote the
2\times 2
matrix over
\Lambda\simeq\mathcal{O}\llbracket X\rrbracket
given by

\begin{bmatrix}a_{p}(g)&1\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\Phi_{n}&0\end{bmatrix}
, where
\Phi_{n}=\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}(1+X)^{jp^{n-1}}
denotes the
p^{n}
-th cyclotomic polynomial in
1+X
.

Theorem C

(Theorems
4.10
and
4.11
)
.

Suppose that (
(H0)
) holds. Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
that are split in
K
. There exist unique elements
z_{m}^{\sharp},z_{m}^{\flat}\in H^{1}(K[m],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}\otimes\Lambda(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})(2))
such that

\begin{bmatrix}z_{n,m}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\textup{res}_{K[mp^{n-1}]/K[mp^{n}]}(z_{n-1,m})\end{bmatrix}=C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{pr}^{\infty}_{n}z_{m}^{\sharp}\\
\mathrm{pr}^{\infty}_{n}z_{m}^{\flat}\end{bmatrix},

where

H^{1}(K[m],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}\otimes\Lambda(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})(2))\xrightarrow{\,\mathrm{pr}^{\infty}_{n}\,}H^{1}(K[m],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})(2))

denotes the natural projection map.
Furthermore, let
q
be a prime coprime to
Nmp
and split in
K
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{cor}_{K[mq]/K[m]}\left(z_{mq}^{\bullet}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{m}^{\bullet}\,,\qquad\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}\,.

1.2.5.

We assume until §
1.2.6
that the residual representation
\overline{M}
is absolutely irreducible (see also our “big image” hypothesis (
(

BI

)
), which is required in Theorem
D
below).

Similar to previous works on non-ordinary Iwasawa theory for
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
, the matrices
C_{g,n}
can be used to define local signed Coleman maps. Their kernels then give rise to signed Selmer groups that we denote by
H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
for
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
. Also, the dual local conditions for the
p
-divisible module
A:=(M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p})/M
give rise to Selmer groups
\mathrm{Sel}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)
, and we denote by
X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)
their Pontryagin duals. We refer the reader to §
5.3
for precise definitions of these objects.

Under the assumption that
a_{p}(g)=0
and that hypothesis (
(H

0

-

)
) (formulated in §
5.1
) holds, we prove in §
5.2
that
z_{1}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
. Based on these constructions, we may formulate the following signed Iwasawa main conjectures, similar to the Heegner-point Iwasawa main conjectures of Perrin-Riou studied in
[
39
]
(see also the supersingular analogue discussed in
[
13
]
).

Conjecture
.

Let
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
. The class
z_{1}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
lies in
H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
. Moreover,
z_{1}^{\bullet}
is not
\Lambda
-torsion, both
H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
and
X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)
are rank-one
\Lambda
-modules and

\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)_{\mathrm{tors}}\right)=\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)}{\Lambda\cdot z_{1}^{\bullet}}\right)^{2}.

The Euler system machinery developed in
[
29
]
implies, under appropriate hypotheses, one inclusion in these main conjectures:

Theorem D

(Theorem
5.15
)
.

Let
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
. Assume that
a_{p}(g)=0
, that hypothesis (
(H

0

-

)
) holds and that the “big image” hypothesis (
(

BI

)
) holds. If
z_{1}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
is not
\Lambda
-torsion, then both
H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
and
X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)
are rank-one
\Lambda
-modules and

\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)_{\mathrm{tors}}\right)\supseteq\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)}{\Lambda\cdot z_{1}^{\bullet}}\right)^{2}.

We refer the reader to Remark
5.9
for a discussion of our assumption
a_{p}(g)=0
.

1.2.6.

In Appendix
B
, we discuss the definite counterpart of our diagonal cycles. Namely, taking
B
to be definite and letting
\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
be the (zero-dimensional) Shimura set associated with
B_{E}
of level
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}
, we define an element
\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}]
by imitating the construction of
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
. Thanks to
[
36
]
, these theta elements satisfy a norm relation identical to that of the diagonal cycles. Moreover, they give rise to an alternative construction of Hsieh’s diagonal theta elements, which were used by Hsieh
[
28
]
to construct balanced triple-product
p
-adic
L
-functions, with the advantage that our approach a priori allows more flexibility in the
p
-adic variation. We explain in §
B.2
how Hsieh’s construction of diagonal theta elements can be reinterpreted within Loeffler’s framework (see Proposition
B.7
and Corollary
B.8
).

In §
B.4
, using the theta elements
\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}]
, we
construct unbounded (resp. bounded signed) balanced
p
-adic
L
-functions in a single variable. Let us give an overview of our construction. Let
f
and
g
be weight-two cusp forms on
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
as above and let
h
be a primitive Hida family on
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
with coefficients in a normal domain
\mathbf{I}
, finite flat over
\Lambda=\mathcal{O}\llbracket 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rrbracket
. Assume that we have a (non-zero) primitive Jacquet–Langlands lift
(f^{B},g^{B},\textup{\bf h}^{B})
to
B
(see (

JL

)
) for a list of conditions that guarantee this). Then, mimicking the definition of
{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)
(cf. §
1.2.1
), we construct elements
\Theta_{n}(f,g)\in e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n}}]
that satisfy the relation

(1.1)

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{n}^{n+1}\,\Theta_{n+1}(f,g)=a_{p}(g)\cdot\Theta_{n}(f,g)-\chi_{g}(p)\cdot\textup{res}_{n-1}^{n}\Theta_{n-1}(f,g)\,

for any integer
n\geq 2
(cf. Proposition
B.12
).
Let
\alpha
and
\beta
denote the roots of the Hecke polynomial of
g
at
p
. Then, for
\xi\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
and
n\geq 2
, (
1.1
) let us show that the
p
-stabilized theta element

\Theta_{n,\xi}(f,g):=\frac{1}{\xi^{n}}\left(\Theta_{n}(f,g)-\frac{\chi_{g}(p)}{\xi}\textup{res}_{n-1}^{n}\Theta_{n-1}(f,g)\right)\,\in\,e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}{\rm Frac}(\mathcal{O})[S_{U_{n}}]

is norm-compatible, in the sense that
{\rm pr}_{n}^{n+1}\Theta_{n+1,\xi}(f,g)=\Theta_{n,\xi}(f,g)
.

Theorem E

(Theorems
B.13
and
B.14
)
.

Let
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathcal{D}_{?}
be as in Definition
4.1
.

Let
\xi\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
be such that
\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\xi)<1
. There exists a unique element

\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g)\in\mathscr{L}\otimes_{\Lambda}\varprojlim_{n}e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n}}]

such that its image under the natural morphism

\mathscr{L}\otimes_{\Lambda}\varprojlim_{n}e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n}}]\longrightarrow e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}{\rm Frac}(\mathcal{O})[S_{U_{n}}]
coincides with
\Theta_{n,\xi}(f,g)
. Let us put
\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g,\textup{\bf h}):=\textup{\bf h}^{B,\dagger}\left(\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g)\right)\in\mathscr{L}\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathbf{I}
, where
\textup{\bf h}^{B,\dagger}
is the self-dual twist of
h

(
cf. Definition
B.9

(\textup{iii}))
. Then,
\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\xi)}
.

There exist unique
\Theta_{\infty}^{\sharp}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})
,
\Theta_{\infty}^{\flat}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\in\mathbf{I}
such that

\begin{bmatrix}\Theta_{\infty,\alpha}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\\
\Theta_{\infty,\beta}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\end{bmatrix}=Q_{g}^{-1}M_{\log,g}\begin{bmatrix}\Theta_{\infty}^{\sharp}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\\
\Theta_{\infty}^{\flat}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\end{bmatrix},

where
Q_{g}
and
M_{\log,g}
are the matrices introduced in Definition
4.7
.

We refer to the elements
\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})
, resp.
\Theta_{\infty}^{\bullet}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})
for
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
, as the unbounded (resp. bounded signed) balanced triple-product
p
-adic
L
-functions. These are trito-non-ordinary versions of Hsieh’s balanced
p
-adic
L
-function
2
2

2

Greenberg and Seveso in
[
22
]
have also constructed balanced triple-product
p
-adic
L
-functions in the non-ordinary scenario. Since they work over wide open discs properly contained in the full weight space, the (essential part of the) denominators can be absorbed in the coefficient ring. Our work emphasises Iwasawa theory and integrality, which requires that we work over the full weight space.
and, thanks to the calculation on trilinear periods in
[
28
, §4]
, they enjoy identical interpolative properties (which characterise them thanks to their indicated growth properties).

1.2.7.

We conclude by noting that the striking parallelism between Hsieh’s construction of theta elements in terms of the special locus on Shimura sets in the definite scenario, and the construction of diagonal cycles in the indefinite setting suggests the existence of a bipartite Euler system (in the sense of Bertolini–Darmon and Howard
[
4
,
26
]
), but we do not explore this point further in the present article.

2.
p
-adic families of diagonal cycles

The goal of this section is to provide a construction of
p
-adic families of diagonal cycles in the triple product of Shimura curves inspired by the construction of the balanced
p
-adic
L
-function of Hsieh in
[
28
]
(see Appendix
B
where, in order to highlight the parallelism, we review the construction of theta elements and
p
-adic
L
-functions therein) and by the general technique proposed by Loeffler in
[
36
]
.

2.1.
Shimura curves and threefolds

Let
N=N^{+}N^{-}
be a positive integer coprime to
p
, where
N^{+}
and
N^{-}
are coprime and
N^{-}
is square-free. Let
B
be the indefinite quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
of discriminant
N^{-}
. We also put
E=\mathbb{Q}^{\oplus 3}
and
B_{E}=B^{\oplus 3}
. Once and for all fix an isomorphism

(2.1)

\Psi=\prod_{q\nmid N^{-}}\Psi_{q}\,:\quad B(\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{(N^{-})})\simeq M_{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{(N^{-})}).

We fix a maximal order
\mathcal{O}_{B}
such that
\Psi_{q}(\mathcal{O}_{B}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{q})=M_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{q})
for all
q\nmid\infty N^{-}
. We then let
U_{0}(N)
be the open compact subgroup of
\widehat{B}^{\times}
defined by the Eichler order
R_{N}
of level
N^{+}
in
\mathcal{O}_{B}
and let

U_{1}(N)=\{g\in U_{0}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{q}(g_{q})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\star&\star\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }N\mathbb{Z}_{q})\text{ for }q|N^{+}\}.

2.1.1.

For a natural number
n
, we put

(2.2)

\displaystyle U_{n}

\displaystyle:=\{g\in U_{1}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\star\\
&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\,,\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}\,,

(2.3)

\displaystyle U_{Z,n}

\displaystyle:=\{g\in U_{1}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\\
&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\,,\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}\,,

(2.4)

\displaystyle U_{0,n}

\displaystyle:=\{g\in U_{1}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\star&\star\\
&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\}\,,

(2.5)

\displaystyle U_{1,n}

\displaystyle:=\{g\in U_{1}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\star&\star\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\}\,.

2.1.2.

Let us denote by
\iota:B\hookrightarrow B_{E}
the diagonal embedding, and let
\mathcal{U}_{i}(N):=U_{i}(N)^{\times 3}\subseteq\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}
for
i=0,1
. For each
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{3}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{1,\mathbf{n}}

\displaystyle:=U_{1,n_{1}}\times U_{1,n_{2}}\times U_{1,n_{3}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{Z,\mathbf{n}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{g\in\mathcal{U}_{1}(N)\,:\,g\equiv\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{1}&\star\\
0&a_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{1}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{2}&\star\\
0&a_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{2}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{3}&\star\\
0&a_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{3}}\right)\,,\,a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{g\in\mathcal{U}_{1}(N)\,:\,g\equiv\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\star\\
0&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{1}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\star\\
0&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{2}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\star\\
0&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{3}}\right)\,,\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\mathbf{n}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{g\in\mathcal{U}_{1}(N)\,:\,g\equiv\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&0\\
\star&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{1}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&0\\
\star&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{2}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&0\\
\star&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{3}}\right)\,,\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}

\displaystyle:=U_{n_{1}}\times U_{n_{2}}\times U_{n_{3}}\,.

When
\mathbf{n}=(n,n,n)
, we write
\mathcal{U}_{\bullet,(n)}
in place of
\mathcal{U}_{\bullet,\mathbf{n}}
for any choice of
\bullet\in\{1,Z,\emptyset,{\rm spl}\}
. Note that
\mathcal{U}_{1,(n)}
and
\mathcal{U}_{(n)}
are normal subgroups of
\mathcal{U}_{Z,(n)}
, and the quotient
\mathcal{U}_{Z,(n)}/\mathcal{U}_{1,(n)}
is isomorphic to
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
.

2.1.3.
Notation

If
\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3}),\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}
, we put
\mathbf{m}+\mathbf{n}:=(m_{1}+n_{1},m_{2}+n_{2},m_{3}+n_{3})
.

2.1.4.

We fix an isomorphism
\epsilon_{\infty}:B\otimes\mathbb{R}\simeq M_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and we let
X(U)
be the Shimura curve over
\mathbb{Q}
of level
U
(where
U
denotes any one of the level subgroups above), whose complex points are given by the double cosets in

B^{\times}\backslash(\mathcal{H}^{\pm}\times\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times}/U),

where
B^{\times}
acts on
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}:=\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
via the isomorphism
\epsilon_{\infty}:(B\otimes\mathbb{R})^{\times}\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
3
3

3

We let
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
act on
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
via the natural left action
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cdot z=\frac{az+b}{cz+d}
, for
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
z\in\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
.
. The Shimura curve
X(U)
is proper unless
N^{-}=1
and, if
N^{+}
is big enough, is a fine moduli space of elliptic curves, resp. false elliptic curves
4
4

4

These are abelian surfaces with an action by
\mathcal{O}_{B}
and level-
U
structures.
, if
N^{-}=1
, resp.
N^{-}\not=1
. This admits an integral refinement, in the sense that
X(U)
has an integral model
\mathscr{X}(U)
over
\mathbb{Z}[1/Np]
, which represents the following moduli problem: to any
\mathbb{Z}[1/Np]
-scheme
S
, we assign the set of isomorphisms classes of
(A/S,\alpha,\eta_{U})
, where
A/S
is an abelian scheme over
S
of relative dimension
2
,
\alpha:\mathcal{O}_{B}\hookrightarrow{\rm End}_{S}(A)
, and
\eta_{U}
is a level-
U
structure on
A/S
.

2.1.5.

Given a sufficiently small open compact subgroup
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}
, we denote by
\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}}
the Shimura threefold over
\mathbb{Q}
of level
\mathcal{U}
. When
\mathcal{U}=U_{1}\times U_{2}\times U_{3}
, the threefold
\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}}
is the product of Shimura curves
X(U_{1})\times X(U_{2})\times X(U_{3})
. The diagonal embedding
\iota:B\hookrightarrow B_{E}
induces a morphism from the Shimura curve to the Shimura threefold as follows. If
U\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times}
is any sufficiently small open compact subgroup which maps into
\iota(\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times})\cap\mathcal{U}
, we have a morphism of varieties over
\mathbb{Q}

\iota_{U,\mathcal{U}}:X(U)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}}\,,

which can be described as
[z,g]\mapsto[((z,z,z),(g,g,g))]
for complex points. When
U=\iota^{-1}(\mathcal{U})
, the morphism
\iota_{U,\mathcal{U}}
is a closed immersion of codimension 2. Finally, recall that right-multiplication by
g\in\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times}
, resp.
g\in\mathcal{O}_{B_{E},f}^{\times}
, induces a morphism of Shimura curves
\mathcal{T}_{g}:X(U)\to X(g^{-1}Ug)
, resp. of Shimura threefolds
\mathcal{T}_{g}:\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}}\to\mathbf{X}_{g^{-1}\mathcal{U}g}
, defined over the reflex field
\mathbb{Q}
.

2.2.
Hecke correspondences

2.2.1.

For a level subgroup
U\subseteq\widehat{B}^{\times}
and any ring
R
, we denote by
\mathcal{H}_{U}(\widehat{B}^{\times},R)
the space of bi-
U
invariant compactly supported
R
-valued functions on
\widehat{B}^{\times}
, endowed with the convolution product
\ast
. When
R=\mathbb{Z}
, we simply denote it by
\mathcal{H}_{U}(\widehat{B}^{\times})
. We define
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{U}}(\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times},R)
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{U}}(\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times})
analogously.

For
U\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times}
as above and
g\in\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times}
, the characteristic function
[UgU]\in\mathcal{H}_{U}(\widehat{B}^{\times})
defines a Hecke correspondence

\textstyle{X(g^{-1}Ug\cap U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\rm pr}
\textstyle{X(U\cap gUg^{-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm pr^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X(U)}
\textstyle{X(U)}

which acts on the cohomology of
X(U)
via
({\rm pr}^{\prime})_{*}\circ(\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}})_{*}\circ({\rm pr})^{*}
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{B,\bullet}^{\rm sph}(Np^{n})
denote the subalgebra of
\mathcal{H}_{U_{\bullet,n}}(\widehat{B}^{\times})
, where
\bullet\in\{\emptyset,1\}
in §
2.2.1
and §
2.2.2
, generated by the characteristic functions
[U_{\bullet,n}\,g\,U_{\bullet,n}]
, with
g=(g_{q})_{q}\in\widehat{B}^{\times}
such that
g_{q}=1
for every
q\mid Np^{n}
. To be more precise, for a prime
\ell
let us consider

\eta_{\ell}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\ell&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
and
s_{\ell}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\ell&\\
&\ell\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\,,

and define

\mathrm{T}_{\ell}^{\prime}:=[U_{\bullet,n}\Psi^{-1}(\eta_{\ell}^{-1})U_{\bullet,n}],\,\quad\mathrm{S}_{\ell}:=[U_{\bullet,n}\Psi^{-1}(s_{\ell})U_{\bullet,n}]\,,

and put
\mathcal{H}_{B,\bullet}^{\rm sph}(Np^{n}):=\mathbb{Z}\left[\mathrm{T}_{\ell}^{\prime},\mathrm{S}_{\ell},\mathrm{S}_{\ell}^{-1},\,\ell\nmid Np^{n}\right]
.

When
n>0
, we further consider the operators
{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}
and
\langle x\rangle
, for
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, defined by

{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}:=[U_{\bullet,n}\Psi^{-1}(\eta_{p}^{-1})U_{\bullet,n}],\,\quad\langle x\rangle:=[U_{\bullet,n}\Psi^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&x\end{smallmatrix}\right)U_{\bullet,n}],

and set
\mathcal{H}_{B,\bullet}(Np^{n}):=\mathcal{H}_{B,\bullet}^{\rm sph}(Np^{n})[{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime},\langle x\rangle,x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}]=\mathbb{Z}\left[\{\mathrm{T}_{\ell}^{\prime},\mathrm{S}_{\ell},\mathrm{S}_{\ell}^{-1},\,\ell\nmid Np^{n}\},\{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime},\langle x\rangle,x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}\right]
.

The algebra of Hecke correspondences generated by
\mathcal{H}_{B,\bullet}(Np^{n})
will be denoted by
\mathrm{T}_{B,\bullet}(Np^{n})
.

2.2.2.

By a slight abuse of notation, we let
\eta_{p}
also denote its image in
(B\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
via
\Psi^{-1}
. We let
X(U_{\bullet,n+1})\xrightarrow{\pi_{i}}X(U_{\bullet,n})
,
i=1,2
, be the two degeneracy maps given as follows: the map
\pi_{1}
is the natural degeneracy map, whereas
\pi_{2}
is given by

X(U_{\bullet,n+1})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}_{\eta_{p}^{-1}}}X(\eta_{p}U_{\bullet,n+1}\eta_{p}^{-1})\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}^{\prime}}X(U_{\bullet,n}),

where
{\rm pr}^{\prime}
denotes the natural degeneracy map associated with the inclusion
\eta_{p}U_{\bullet,n+1}\eta_{p}^{-1}\subseteq U_{\bullet,n}
. These projections factor through the Shimura curve of level
U_{\bullet,n}\cap U_{0,n+1}
as follows. We have a commutative diagram (for
i=1,2
)

\textstyle{X(U_{\bullet,n+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{i}}
\textstyle{X(U_{\bullet,n}\cap U_{0,n+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi_{i}}
\textstyle{X(U_{\bullet,n})}

where
X(U_{\bullet,n+1})\xrightarrow{\mu}X(U_{\bullet,n}\cap U_{0,n+1})
denotes the natural projection, and
\varpi_{2}
is defined by composing the natural degeneracy map with
\mathcal{T}_{\eta_{p}^{-1}}
. When we want to emphasize the levels involved, we denote
\pi_{1}
by
{\rm pr}^{n+1}_{n}
.

We record the following statement from
[
30
]
, which will be used in the proof of the
Q
-system relations for the diagonal cycles.

Proposition 2.1

(
[
30
, Proposition 2.4.5]
)
.

As morphisms

H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{\bullet,1}),\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))\to H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{1}(N)),\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j))

for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
we have

\displaystyle\pi_{2,*}\circ{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=p\cdot\pi_{1,*}

\displaystyle\pi_{1,*}\circ{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{T}_{p}^{\prime}\circ\pi_{1,*}-\mathrm{S}_{p}^{-1}\circ\pi_{2,*}.

2.2.3.

For
\bullet\in\{\emptyset,1,{\rm spl}\}
and a triple
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
of integers as above, we let
\mathcal{H}_{B_{E},\bullet}^{\rm sph}(Np^{\mathbf{n}})
denote the subalgebra of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{U}_{\bullet,\mathbf{n}}}(\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times})
generated by the characteristic functions
[\mathcal{U}_{\bullet,\mathbf{n}}\,\underline{g}\,\mathcal{U}_{\bullet,\mathbf{n}}]
, with
\underline{g}\in\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}
such that
\underline{g_{q}}=1
for every
q\mid Np
. We define

\displaystyle\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}:=[\mathcal{U}_{\bullet,\mathbf{n}}\iota(\eta_{p}^{-1})\mathcal{U}_{\bullet,\mathbf{n}}]\,.

For
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
and
g\in(B\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
, we let
{}^{i}g,{}^{ij}g\in(B_{E}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
denote the elements that equal to
g
in position
i
, resp. in positions
i
and
j
, and to the identity element otherwise. Then we let
{}^{i}\mathbf{U}_{p}^{\prime}
and
{}^{ij}\mathbf{U}_{p}^{\prime}
be the operators associated to
{}^{i}\eta_{p}^{-1}
and
{}^{ij}\eta_{p}^{-1}
, respectively. Finally, if
x,y,z\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, we let
\langle x,y,z\rangle
be the operator attached to the element
\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&x\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&y\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&z\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)
, and set
\langle\mathbb{x}\rangle:=\langle x,x,x\rangle
.

Let us define

\mathcal{H}_{B_{E},\bullet}(Np^{\mathbf{n}}):=\mathcal{H}_{B_{E},\bullet}^{\rm sph}(Np^{\mathbf{n}})[\{\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime},{}^{i}\mathbf{U}_{p}^{\prime},{}^{ij}\mathbf{U}_{p}^{\prime},\quad i,j\in\{1,2,3\}\},\{\langle x,y,z\rangle,\quad\,x,y,z\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}\rangle]

and let
\mathrm{T}_{B_{E},\bullet}(Np^{\mathbf{n}})
be the algebra of Hecke correspondences generated by
\mathcal{H}_{B_{E},\bullet}(Np^{\mathbf{n}})
.

2.2.4.
Compatibility of Hecke actions

For
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
, let us denote by

\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\xrightarrow{\,{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}\,}\mathbf{X}_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}}=\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}:=X({U_{n_{1}}})\times X({U_{n_{2}}})\times X({U_{n_{3}}})

the natural projection map.

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
T
, resp.
\widetilde{T}
, be the Hecke correspondences in
\mathrm{T}_{B_{E}}(Np^{\mathbf{n}})
, resp.
\mathrm{T}_{B_{E},{\rm spl}}(Np^{\mathbf{n}})
, associated with
g\in\mathcal{O}_{B_{E},f}^{\times}
. Then

{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}^{*}\circ T=\widetilde{T}\circ{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}^{*},\text{ and }{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}\circ\widetilde{T}=T\circ{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}.

Proof.

The Hecke correspondences
T
and
\widetilde{T}
are given by

\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\xleftarrow{{\rm pr}}\mathbf{X}_{g^{-1}\,\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}\,g\,\,\cap\,\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}}}\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}},

\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\xleftarrow{\widetilde{\rm pr}}\mathbf{X}_{g^{-1}\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}\,g\,\,\cap\,\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}}}\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}.

We claim that the result follows from the existence of a finite étale morphism
\psi:\mathbf{X}_{g^{-1}\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}g\cap\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\to\mathbf{X}_{g^{-1}\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}g\cap\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}}
such that

\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{g^{-1}\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}g\cap\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{g^{-1}\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}g\cap\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\rm pr}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}}

is commutative and has Cartesian squares.
Indeed, if that is the case, we have

\displaystyle{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}^{*}\circ\widetilde{T}

\displaystyle={\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}^{*}\circ(\widetilde{\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}})_{*}\circ\widetilde{\rm pr}^{*}

\displaystyle=({\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}})_{*}\circ\psi^{*}\circ\widetilde{\rm pr}^{*}

\displaystyle=({\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{*}\circ{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}^{*}

\displaystyle=T\circ{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}^{*},

where the second equality follows from the Cartesianness of the right square, while the third follows from the commutativity of the left square. Similarly,

\displaystyle{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}\circ T

\displaystyle={\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}\circ({\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{*}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}})_{*}\circ\psi_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{*}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{T}_{g^{-1}})_{*}\circ\widetilde{\rm pr}^{*}\circ{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{T}\circ{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast},

where the second equality follows from the commutativity of the right square, while the third follows from Cartesianness of the left square.
We are left to show that such a
\psi
exists. As the level subgroups
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}
and
\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}
differ only at
p
, the existence of
\psi
is easily seen for any
g
with trivial
p
-component. If
T
and
\tilde{T}
are given by the diamond operator
\langle x,y,z\rangle
, with
x,y,z\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
,
\psi
can be taken to be
{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}
(in this case all the horizontal arrows of the diagram above are isomorphisms). We now verify the existence of
\psi
for
g=\iota(\eta_{p}^{-1})
; the other cases follow similarly. When
g=\iota(\eta_{p}^{-1})
, the
p
-component of
V:=g^{-1}\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}g\cap\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}
, resp.
U:=g^{-1}\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}g\cap\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}
, is

V_{p}=\left\{(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n},p}\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{i})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\star&0\\
\star&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p)\,\,\,\forall i\right\},

resp.

U_{p}=\left\{(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\in\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n},p}\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{i})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\star&0\\
\star&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p)\,\,\,\forall i\right\}.

Let
\psi:\mathbf{X}_{V}\to\mathbf{X}_{U}
denote the natural projection induced by the inclusion
V\subseteq U
. It is a Galois cover with Galois group isomorphic to
\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}/\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}
. The commutativity of the diagram above is immediate. The Cartesianness of the two squares follows from the fact
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}\backslash\mathcal{U}_{{\rm spl},\mathbf{n}}/U
is a singleton and
U\cap\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}=V
.
∎

2.3.
Modified diagonal cycles

To define the regularized diagonal cycles, we define the element

u:=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{3}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\,.

2.3.1.

This choice of
u
is motivated by the following observation. Let
Q
, resp.
\overline{Q}
,
denote the upper-triangular, resp. lower-triangular, Borel parabolic of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and let
\mathrm{GL}_{2}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
act on the left on the flag variety
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{3}/\overline{Q}
. It is well-known that it acts with an open orbit, whose stabilizer is the center
Z
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
. A representative of this orbit can then be chosen to be
u
5
5

5

Note that this flag variety can be identified with
3
-copies of
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, allowing each copy of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
to act on the
right
as Möbius transformations, so that the lower triangular Borel subgroup is the stabilizer of
[1:0]
. The point in the flag variety
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
that corresponds to
u
is then

[1:0]u:=\left([1:0]\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),[1:0]\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),[1:0]\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=\left([1:1],[1:0],[0:1]\right)\,.

The left action of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
on the flag variety (once it is identified by
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
as above) is given by coordinate-wise Möbius transformations. In particular, for
g=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, we have

g\cdot u=\left([a+b:c+d],[a:c],[b:d]\right)\in\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\,.

It is then clear that the stabilizer of
u
under this action is the center of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and that the orbit of
u
is indeed open.
. This observation will allow us to employ the machinery developed in
[
36
]
to show that the images of the Shimura curves inside their triple product twisted by right multiplication by
u
are norm compatible.

Definition 2.3
.

Let us consider the following Shimura curves and threefolds:

•

X_{Z,n}:=X(U_{Z,n})
, where we recall that

U_{Z,n}=\{g\in U_{0,n}\,:\,g\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\\
&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,\mod p^{n}\}
,

•

\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
and
\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}^{\mathbf{n}}}
are the Shimura threefolds associated with the level subgroups
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\mathbf{n}}

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in\mathcal{U}_{1}(N)\,:\,g\equiv\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&0\\
\star&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{1}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&0\\
\star&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{2}},\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&0\\
\star&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mod p^{n_{3}}\right)\,,\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\right\}.

As before, we write
(n)
in place of the triple
(n,n,n)
, and
\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}
in place of
\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n,n,n)}}
(similarly for
\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}
). Note that right multiplication by
\iota(\eta_{p}^{\mathbf{n}})
(where
\eta_{p}^{\mathbf{n}}:=(\eta_{p}^{n_{1}},\eta_{p}^{n_{2}},\eta_{p}^{n_{3}})
) induces an isomorphism
\mathcal{T}_{\iota(\eta_{p}^{\mathbf{n}})}:\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}^{\mathbf{n}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
. We start with the following auxiliary lemma.

Lemma 2.4
.

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
u\,\mathcal{U}^{(n)}u^{-1}\cap\iota(\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times})=U_{Z,{n}}
.

Proof.

The
p
-component of an element
g=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
c_{1}&d_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{2}&b_{2}\\
c_{2}&d_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{3}&b_{3}\\
c_{3}&d_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\in u\,\mathcal{U}^{(n)}u^{-1}
reduces modulo
p^{n}
to

\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\\
c_{1}&a\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a+c_{2}&-c_{2}\\
c_{2}&a-c_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&-c_{3}\\
&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right).

Then
g
belongs to
\iota(\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times})
if and only if
c_{i}\equiv 0
modulo
p^{n}
. This shows the desired equality.
∎

2.3.2.

By Lemma
2.4
, we obtain closed immersions

\displaystyle\iota_{n}^{u}\,:\quad X_{n}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}\,,

given by the composition of the diagonal embedding
\iota_{U_{Z,n}\,,\,u\mathcal{U}^{(n)}u^{-1}}
with
\mathcal{T}_{u}
.

Lemma 2.5
.

For any positive integer
n
, we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n+1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{n}^{E}}
\textstyle{X_{Z,n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi_{Z,n}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{n+1}^{u}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}_{\circ}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi_{n}^{E}}
\textstyle{X_{Z,n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{n}^{u}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}}

where
\mathcal{U}^{(n)}_{\circ}=\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\,\cap\,\iota(\eta_{p})\,\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\iota(\eta_{p})^{-1}
, and all the vertical maps are the natural projections, with Cartesian bottom square.

Proof.

The commutativity of the triangle in the diagram is obvious. The horizontal arrows are closed immersions because of Lemma
2.4
and the equality

u\,\mathcal{U}^{(n+1)}u^{-1}\cap\iota(\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times})=u\,\mathcal{U}^{(n)}_{\circ}u^{-1}\cap\iota(\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times}).

The claim that the bottom square is Cartesian thus follows from the fact that the degrees of the vertical maps
\varpi_{Z,n}
and
\varpi_{n}^{E}
are both
p^{3}
(in particular, they agree). Note that both equalities, which can be checked directly, follow from
[
36
, Lemma 4.4.1]
as
u\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{3}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
satisfies the hypotheses (A), (B) of
[
36
, §4.3]
, with
\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{3}/\overline{Q}
,
Q^{0}_{H}=\mathrm{GL}_{2}
, and
\overline{Q}^{0}_{G}=Z\cdot\overline{N}
, where
Z
and
\overline{N}
denote the center of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
and the unipotent radical of
\overline{Q}
respectively.
∎

2.3.3.

Let

{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{r}}_{\mathbf{n}}:{\rm CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n+r}}})\to{\rm CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}})\,,\qquad\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}),\,\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{3}

denote the pushforward maps induced from the natural degeneracy map
\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n+r}}}\xrightarrow{}\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
. Let us also denote by

\mathbf{1}_{n}=\sum_{\mathcal{C}\in\pi_{0}(X_{Z,n})}[\mathcal{C}]\in{\rm CH}^{0}(X_{Z,n})

the fundamental cycle of
X_{Z,n}
, where
\pi_{0}(X_{Z,n})
denotes the set of connected components of
X_{Z,n}
. Moreover, we let
\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\star}
be the pushforward of the isomorphism
\mathcal{T}_{\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})}:\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}\to\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}
.

Definition 2.6
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we define
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}:=\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}\circ\iota_{n,\ast}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n})\in{\rm CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}})
.

For every
{\mathbf{n}}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
, we define
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}:={\rm pr}^{(n)}_{\mathbf{n}}\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}
, where
n=\max\{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\}
.

Remark 2.7
.

By definition, the class
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}
is represented by the image of the codimension-2 cycle
X_{Z,n}
inside
\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}
, via the embedding given by
\mathcal{T}_{\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})}\,\circ\,\mathcal{T}_{u}\,\circ\,\iota_{U_{Z,n},u\mathcal{U}^{(n)}u^{-1}}
. Note that this embedding sends
[(\tau,g)]\in X_{Z,n}
, with
\tau\in\mathbb{C}\smallsetminus\mathbb{R}
and
g\in\widehat{B}^{\times}
, to the element

[(\tau,\tau,\tau),\left(g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{n}&1\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{n}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-p^{n}&\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)]\in\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}.

Our choice of the twisting of the diagonal embedding is therefore concurrent with Hsieh’s construction of diagonal theta elements in the definite case (cf. Proposition
B.7
).

Proposition 2.8
.

For every triple
{\mathbf{n}}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
of positive integers, let us put

\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}=(n_{1}+1,n_{2}+1,n_{3}+1)\,.

We then have

{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n+1}}})=\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}.

Proof.

This follows from
[
36
, Proposition 4.5.2]
, and we give a sketch of the proof for the convenience of the reader. We first claim that it is enough to verify the statement when
{\mathbf{n}}=(n)
. Indeed, if we suppose that and let
n=\max\{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\}
, we have

{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}={\rm pr}^{(n)}_{\mathbf{n}}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{(n)}.

This implies that

\displaystyle{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n+1}}})

\displaystyle={\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n+1)}})

\displaystyle={\rm pr}^{(n)}_{\mathbf{n}}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{(n)}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n+1)}})

\displaystyle={\rm pr}^{(n)}_{\mathbf{n}}\circ\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{\mathbf{n}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}},

where the fourth equality follows from the fact that
\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}
commutes with the pushforward maps
{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{\mathbf{n}}
(cf.
[
36
]
, Proposition 4.5.3).

We now prove the formula in the case where
{\mathbf{n}}=(n)
. By Lemma
2.5
,

(\varpi_{n}^{E})^{\ast}\circ\iota_{n,\ast}^{u}=(\mu_{n}^{E})_{\ast}\,\circ\,\iota_{n+1,\ast}^{u}\,\circ\,\varpi_{Z,n}^{\ast}.

As the formation of the fundamental class
\mathbf{1}_{n}
is compatible with base-change, we have

(\varpi_{n}^{E})^{*}\circ\iota_{n,*}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n})=(\mu_{n}^{E})_{*}\,\circ\,\iota_{n+1,*}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n+1}).

Applying the pushforward of

\varpi_{2,n}^{E}:\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{(n)}}\xrightarrow{\,\mathcal{T}_{\iota(\eta_{p})}\,}\mathbf{X}_{\iota(\eta_{p}^{-1})\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{(n)}\iota(\eta_{p})}\xrightarrow{\,{\rm pr}\,}\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}\,,

where
{\rm pr}
is the natural degeneracy map, we deduce that

\varpi_{2,n,\ast}^{E}\circ(\varpi_{n}^{E})^{*}\,\circ\,\iota_{n,*}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n})=\varpi_{2,n,\ast}^{E}\,\circ\,(\mu_{n}^{E})_{*}\,\circ\,\iota_{n+1,*}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n+1}).

Since
\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}=\varpi_{2,n,\ast}^{E}\,\circ\,(\varpi_{n}^{E})^{*}
, the final equality reads

\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,\iota_{n,*}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n})=\varpi_{2,n,\ast}^{E}\circ(\mu_{n}^{E})_{*}\circ\iota_{n+1,*}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n+1}).

We now apply
\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}
to both sides on the left. While this commutes with
\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}
, an easy matrix computation shows that

\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}\,\circ\,\varpi_{2,n,\ast}^{E}\,\circ\,(\mu_{n}^{E})_{*}={\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{(n)}\,\circ\,\iota(\eta_{p}^{n+1})_{\ast}\,.

This shows that

\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\circ\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}\,\circ\,\iota_{n,*}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n})={\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{(n)}\,\circ\,\iota(\eta_{p}^{n+1})_{\ast}\,\circ\,\iota_{n+1,*}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n+1})\,,

as desired.
∎

For every
{\mathbf{n}}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
, recall that we have set

\mathbf{X}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\xrightarrow{\,{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}\,}\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}=\mathbf{X}_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}}:=X({U_{n_{1}}})\times X({U_{n_{2}}})\times X({U_{n_{3}}})

to denote the natural projection map.

Definition 2.9
.

We put

\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}:={\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}},

and we let (with a slight abuse of notation)

{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{r}}_{\mathbf{n}}\,:\,{\rm CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n+r}})\longrightarrow{\rm CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}})

denote the pushforward of the natural degeneracy map.

Corollary 2.10
.

For every triple
{\mathbf{n}}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
of positive integers, we have

{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}(\Delta_{{\mathbf{n+1}}})=\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}.

Proof.

Since
{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}\circ{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1},\ast}={\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}\circ{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}
, Proposition
2.8
yields

\displaystyle{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}(\Delta_{{\mathbf{n+1}}})={\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}\circ{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1},\ast}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n+1}}})={\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}\circ{\rm pr}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n+1}}})={\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}\circ\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\circ{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},\ast}(\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,\Delta_{\mathbf{n}},

where the penultimate equality follows from Proposition
2.2
.
∎

2.4.
p
-adic cycle class maps

Given an algebraic variety
Y
defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, let us put
\overline{Y}:={Y}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
. Suppose that
X
is an algebraic threefold defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. We denote by

{\rm CH}^{2}_{0}(X;K):=\ker\left({\rm CH}^{2}(X;K)\xrightarrow{{\rm cl}_{0}}H^{4}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}\left(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2)\right)^{G_{K}}\right)

the group of null-homologous algebraic cycles defined over an algebraic number field
K
(which is the kernel of the cycle class map
{\rm cl}_{0}
). We let
{\rm CH}^{2}_{0}(X;K)_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
denote its
p
-adic completion.

Since the target of
{\rm cl}_{0}
is
p
-adically complete,
{\rm cl}_{0}
factors through the
p
-adic completion
{\rm CH}^{2}(X;K)_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
of
{\rm CH}^{2}(X;K)
, and we denote the induced map on
{\rm CH}^{2}(X;K)_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
still by
{\rm cl}_{0}
. Moreover, since the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
H^{4}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}\left(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2)\right)
is torsion-free
6
6

6

Note that
\mathbb{Z}
acts on
H^{4}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2))
via its image under the canonical injection
\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
, it follows that
{\rm im}({\rm cl}_{0})
is also torsion-free, hence it is flat. It then follows from
[
44
,
Lemma 0315
]
that

{\rm CH}^{2}_{0}(X;K)_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}=\ker\left({\rm CH}^{2}(X;K)_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\xrightarrow{{\rm cl}_{0}}H^{4}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}\left(\overline{X},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2)\right)\right)\,.

2.4.1.

We return to the setting of our paper. Let
\mathbb{T}^{B}_{n}
denote the
p
-adic completion of the Hecke algebra
\mathrm{T}_{B}(Np^{n})
, acting on
H^{\ast}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}({X}(U_{n})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Then
\mathbb{T}^{B}_{n}
is a finite
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra and it follows from
[
25
, §7.2, Lemma 1]
that
e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}:=\lim({\rm U}_{p}^{\prime})^{n!}
belongs to
\mathbb{T}^{B}_{n}
. Similarly, we denote by
\mathbb{T}^{B_{E}}_{\mathbf{n}}
the
p
-adic completion of
\mathrm{T}_{B_{E}}(Np^{\mathbf{n}})
, which is isomorphic to the completed tensor product of the
\mathbb{T}^{B}_{n_{i}}
’s, where
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
.

Note that
\mathbb{T}^{B_{E}}_{\mathbf{n}}
acts on
{\rm CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}};K)_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, and as a result, so does
(e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}},{\rm id},e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}})
. This allows us to define

(2.6)

\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}^{\circ}:=(e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}},{\rm T}_{q}^{\prime}-(q+1),e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}})\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}\in{\rm CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}};\mathbb{Q})_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\,,

where
q\nmid Np
is an auxiliary prime (whose choice will not concern us for the moment).

Lemma 2.11
.

We have
\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}^{\circ}\in{\rm CH}^{2}_{0}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}};\mathbb{Q})_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.

Proof.

The argument we present closely follows the proof of
[
18
, Lemma 2.5]
. The correspondence
\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{q}
) acts as multiplication by
p
(resp.
q+1
) on

H^{2}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}({X}(U_{n_{i}})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2))
.
Therefore Hida’s idempotent
e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}
and
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{q}-(q+1)
annihilate this module. As a result,
(e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime}-(q+1),e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}})
annihilates all the direct summands that appear in the Künneth decomposition of
H^{4}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2))
, therefore also
H^{4}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2))
itself, which is the target of the Hecke equivariant map
{\rm cl}_{0}
(defined as in
[
38
, §1.2]
and denoted by
cl_{0,X}
in op. cit.). This concludes the proof of our lemma.
∎

2.4.2.

Thanks to Lemma
2.11
, we may put

(2.7)

\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}:={\rm cl}_{1}(\Delta_{{\mathbf{n}}}^{\circ})\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2)))\,.

By a slight abuse of notation, we will also denote by
\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}
the image of this cohomology class under the compositum

\displaystyle(e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime}-(q+1),e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}})\cdot{\rm CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}};\mathbb{Q})_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\,\subset{\rm CH}^{2}_{0}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}};\mathbb{Q})_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\xrightarrow{{\rm cl}_{1}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},(e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}},{\rm id},e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}})\cdot H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2)))

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt\xrightarrow{\,\,\texttt{K\"{u}}\,\,}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n_{1}})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n_{2}})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n_{3}})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})(2))

where the final projection
Kü
is induced from the Künneth decomposition.

2.4.3.

Let
g\in S_{2}^{B}(U_{1}(N))
be an eigenform that is residually irreducible at
p
. Let us choose forever a prime
q
so that
C_{q}:=a_{q}(g)-(q+1)
is a
p
-adic unit (which is possible thanks to the residual irreducibility hypothesis). There exists a Galois equivariant surjective morphism

{\rm pr}_{g}\,:\,H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{1}(N))\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\twoheadrightarrow V_{g}^{*},

where
V_{g}^{*}
is the
2
-dimensional
p
-adic Galois representation associated to
g
(i.e. an irreducible
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-constituent of the maximal
g
-Hecke-isotypic quotient of
H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{1}(N))\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
), and let
T_{g}^{*}
denote the Galois-stable
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice obtained as the image of
H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{1}(N))\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
under
{\rm pr}_{g}
. For a triple
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
of natural numbers, we define the class

\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}^{\ddagger}:=C_{q}^{-1}((\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n_{1}},{\rm id},(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n_{3}})\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}\,.

We set

H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\circ\,g\,\circ}:=e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n_{1}})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes T_{g}^{*}\otimes e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n_{3}})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})

and define the class

\Delta_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\circ\,g\,\circ}(2))

as the image of the class

\Delta_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}^{\ddagger}

under the morphism induced from

\displaystyle(e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}},{\rm id},e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}})\cdot H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(2))\,\xrightarrow{({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})}H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\circ\,g\,\circ}(2)\,.

composed with
Kü
. Note then that
\Delta_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)
does not depend on the choice of the auxiliary prime
q
.

2.4.4.

In our argument below to prove that diagonal cycles form a
Q
-system (in the sense of Perrin-Riou), we will also need the following modification: For
g
as above and a pair
(n_{1},n_{3})
of positive integers, let us define

\widetilde{\Delta}_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}({\mathbf{X}}_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\circ\,g\,\circ}(2))

as the image of the class
\Delta_{n_{1},1,n_{3}}^{\ddagger}
under the morphism induced from

\displaystyle(e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}},{\rm id},e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}})\cdot H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}({\mathbf{X}}_{n_{1},1,n_{3}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\,\xrightarrow{({\rm id},\,{\,\rm pr}_{g}\,\circ\,\pi_{2,*},\,{\rm id})}H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}({\mathbf{X}}_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\circ\,g\,\circ}\,.

2.4.5.

The following proposition will be used to establish that the collection of diagonal cycles forms a
Q
-system. In what follows, we shall write
\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)
in place of
\Delta_{n,0,n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)
. We similarly put
\Delta_{n}^{\ddagger}:=\Delta_{n,0,n}^{\ddagger}
,
\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}:=\Delta_{n,0,n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}
, and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g):=\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,0,n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)
.

Proposition 2.12
.

The following relation holds for all positive integers
n
:

(\pi_{1},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\Delta_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)=a_{p}(g)\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)-\chi_{g}(p)\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g).

Proof.

This follows from a direct calculation:

\displaystyle(\pi_{1},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\Delta_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)

\displaystyle=({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{n+1,0,n+1}_{n,0,n}\Delta_{n+1}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{n+1,0,n+1}_{n,0,n}\circ(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n-1}\Delta_{n+1,0,n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{n+1,0,n+1}_{n,0,n}\circ(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n-1}{\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{n+1,0,n+1}\Delta_{(n+1)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

(2.8)

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{n+1,0,n+1}_{n,0,n}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{n+1,0,n+1}(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n-1}\Delta_{(n+1)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{n,0,n}(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n-1}\Delta_{(n+1)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,0,n}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{(n)}(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n-1}\Delta_{(n+1)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

(2.9)

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,0,n}\circ(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n-1}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n+1)}_{(n)}\Delta_{(n+1)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

(Corollary
2.10
)

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,0,n}\circ(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n-1}(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,0,n}\circ(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n}({\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id})\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,0,n}\circ({\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id})\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle=({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{n,1,n}_{n,0,n}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,1,n}\circ({\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id})\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}

(2.10)

\displaystyle=({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{n,1,n}_{n,0,n}\circ({\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,1,n}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}

(2.11)

\displaystyle=({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\mathrm{T}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{n,1,n}_{n,0,n}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,1,n}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\mathrm{S}_{p}^{-1},{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,1,n}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle=({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\mathrm{T}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,0,n}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\mathrm{S}_{p}^{-1},{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,1,n}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle=a_{p}(g)\cdot\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)-\chi_{g}(p)\cdot\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)\,.

Here, (
2.8
), (
2.9
), and (
2.10
) are valid since
{\rm pr}^{j}_{j-1}\,\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}=\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,{\rm pr}^{j}_{j-1}
whenever
j>1
, whereas (
2.11
) holds because
{\rm pr}^{1}_{0}\,\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}_{p}^{\prime}\,{\rm pr}^{1}_{0}-\mathrm{S}_{p}^{-1}\,(\pi_{2})_{*}
(cf. Proposition
2.1
).
∎

Remark 2.13
.

A more general form of the asserted identity in Proposition
2.12
holds: for any pair
(n_{1},n_{3})
of positive integers, we have

(\pi_{1},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\Delta_{n_{1}+1,0,n_{3}+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)=a_{p}(g)\Delta_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)-\chi_{g}(p)\widetilde{\Delta}_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)\,.

The proof of this generalization is virtually identical to that of Proposition
2.12
. Since we do not need this extension in what follows, we leave the details to the interested reader.

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
n\geq 2
be an integer. Then,

(\pi_{1},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)=p\cdot{\Delta}_{n-1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)\,.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is similar to that of Proposition
2.12
, and it follows from a direct calculation:

\displaystyle(\pi_{1},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)

\displaystyle=({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{n-1,0,n-1}\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,1,n}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{n-1,0,n-1}\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,1,n}\circ(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n}\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{n,1,n}_{n-1,1,n-1}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{n,1,n}\circ(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n}\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n-1)}_{n-1,1,n-1}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{(n-1)}\circ\,(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n}\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n-1)}_{n-1,1,n-1}\circ\,(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n}\,{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{(n-1)}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

(Corollary
2.10
)

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n-1)}_{n-1,1,n-1}\circ\,(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n}(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})\,\Delta_{(n-1)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=C_{q}^{-1}({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n-1)}_{n-1,1,n-1}\circ\,(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n+1}({\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id})\,\Delta_{(n-1)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}

\displaystyle=({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n-1)}_{n-1,1,n-1}\circ\,({\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id})\,\Delta_{(n-1)}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle=({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ({\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n-1)}_{n-1,1,n-1}\,\Delta_{(n-1)}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle=p\cdot({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{1},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n-1)}_{n-1,1,n-1}\,\Delta_{(n-1)}^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle=p\cdot({\rm id},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ{\rm pr}^{(n-1)}_{n-1,0,n-1}\,\Delta_{(n-1)}^{\ddagger}=p\cdot\Delta_{n-1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)\,.

Here, the fifth and ninth equalities are valid since
{\rm pr}^{j}_{j-1}\,\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}=\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,{\rm pr}^{j}_{j-1}
whenever
j>1
, while the 10th holds because
\pi_{2,*}\,\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}=p\,\pi_{1,*}
.
∎

2.4.6.

Let
f
be an eigenform on
B
of level
U_{1}
given as in (
2.2
). We assume that
f
is
\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}
-ordinary and we define the Carayol–Deligne Galois representation
V_{f}^{*}
(and the lattice
T_{f}^{*}\subset V_{f}^{*}
) as well as a projection
{\rm pr}_{f}
in a manner analogous to §
2.4.3
. We set

H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{1,0,n}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{f\,g\,\circ}:=T_{f}^{*}\otimes T_{g}^{*}\otimes e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\,.

Definition 2.15
.

We set

\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g):=({\rm pr}_{f},\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\circ({\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{1,0,n})\,\,\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{1,0,n}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{f\,g\,\circ}(2))

\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g):=({\rm pr}_{f},\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\circ({\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{1,0,n})\,\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{1,0,n}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{f\,g\,\circ}(2))\,.

Corollary 2.16
.

For any positive integer
n
, we have

(2.12)

({\rm id},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\Delta_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)=a_{p}(g)\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)-\chi_{g}(p)\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)\,,

(2.13)

({\rm id},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)=p\cdot\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)\,.

Proof.

We apply
({\rm pr}_{f},\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\circ({\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{1,0,n})
to both sides of the asserted equality in Proposition
2.12
, to see that

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{f},\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\circ({\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{1,0,n})\circ(\pi_{1},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\Delta_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)

\displaystyle\,=a_{p}(g)({\rm pr}_{f},\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\circ({\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{1,0,n})\,\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\chi_{g}(p)({\rm pr}_{f},\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\circ({\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{1,0,n})\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)

\displaystyle\,=a_{p}(g)\Delta_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)-\chi_{g}(p)\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g).

The proof of (
2.12
) follows from noting that

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{f},\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\circ({\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{1,0,n})\circ(\pi_{1},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\Delta_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)

\displaystyle=\,({\rm pr}_{f},\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\circ({\rm id},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\circ\,({\rm pr}^{n+1,0,n+1}_{1,0,n+1})\,\Delta_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)

\displaystyle\,=({\rm id},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\circ\,({\rm pr}_{f},\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})\circ\,({\rm pr}^{n+1,0,n+1}_{1,0,n+1})\,\Delta_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(g)

\displaystyle\,=:({\rm id},{\rm id},\pi_{1})_{*}\,\circ\,\Delta_{n+1}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)\,.

The proof of (
2.13
) can be deduced from Lemma
2.14
in a similar fashion.
∎

2.4.7.

We recall from §
2.2.3
the diamond operator
\langle x,y,z\rangle
attached to the element
\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&x\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&y\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&z\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)
for
x,y,z\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.

Proposition 2.17
.

Let
n\geq 2
be an integer. For any integer
d\equiv 1\mod p^{n-1}
, we have

\langle 1,1,d\rangle\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)=\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)\,.

Proof.

We recall that

\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)=({\rm pr}_{f},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{1,1,n}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}\,.

Hence, by obvious commutation relations (including
\pi_{2,*}\circ\pi_{1,*}=\pi_{1,*}\circ\pi_{2,*}
), we have

(2.14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \langle 1,1,d^{-1}\rangle\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)=({\rm pr}_{f},\,{\rm pr}_{g}\,,\,{\rm id})\circ({\rm id},\pi_{1},{\rm id})_{*}\circ\,{\rm pr}^{n-1,n-1,n}_{1,1,n}\circ\langle 1,1,d^{-1}\rangle\circ(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}\,.\end{aligned}

Recall also that

\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}:=C_{q}^{-1}(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n}\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}\,,

so that, since
({\rm U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime})
commutes with diamond action and
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}
(note that
n\geq 2
),

(2.15)

\langle 1,1,d^{-1}\rangle\circ(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\ddagger}=C_{q}^{-1}(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},{\rm id},\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n}\langle 1,1,d^{-1}\rangle\circ(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}\,.

Combining (
2.14
) and (
2.15
), it therefore suffices (in order to check the claimed equality in the first part of our proposition) to prove that

(2.16)

\langle 1,1,d^{-1}\rangle\circ(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}=(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}\,.

Furthermore, as we have (by definition)

(2.17)

\Delta_{(n)}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}:={\rm cl}_{1}\circ(e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}},\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime}-(q+1),e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}})\cdot\,\Delta_{(n)}

and
\langle 1,1,d^{-1}\rangle\circ(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}
commutes with the
cycle class map
{\rm cl}_{1}
as well as the ordinary projector (we use the fact that
n\geq 2
once again), it suffices to show that

(2.18)

\langle 1,1,d^{-1}\rangle\circ(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}=(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}\,.

Since
d\equiv 1\pmod{p^{n-1}}
, the elements
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&(d-1)p^{1-n}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
belong to
U_{n-1}
, and hence

\displaystyle\langle 1,1,d^{-1}\rangle\circ(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&(d-1)p^{1-n}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)_{*}(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},{\rm id})_{*}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&(d-1)p^{-n}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)_{*}\,\Delta_{(n)}.

Moreover, as
\Delta_{(n)}={\rm b}_{(n),*}\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}
, the sought-after equality (
2.18
) follows (thanks to Proposition
2.2
) once we show that

(2.19)

\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&(d-1)p^{-n}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)_{*}\,\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}=\,\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}\,.

By Definition
2.6
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&(d-1)p^{-n}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)_{*}\,\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&(d-1)p^{-n}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)_{*}\circ\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}\circ\iota_{n,\ast}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n})

\displaystyle=\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}\circ\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&d-1\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
