&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)_{*}\circ\iota_{n,\ast}^{u}(\mathbf{1}_{n})

\displaystyle=\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}\circ u_{*}\circ\iota\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)_{*}\circ\iota_{U_{Z,n}\,,\,u\,\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\,u^{-1},\ast}(\mathbf{1}_{n})

\displaystyle=\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}\circ u_{*}\circ\iota_{U_{Z,n}\,,\,u\,\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\,u^{-1},\ast}(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{*}\mathbf{1}_{n})\,.

Recall that

\mathbf{1}_{n}=\sum_{\mathcal{C}\in\pi_{0}(X_{Z,n})}[\mathcal{C}]\in{\rm CH}^{0}(X_{Z,n})

is the fundamental cycle of
X_{Z,n}
.
Since
d\equiv 1\mod p^{n-1}
and
n>1
, it follows that
d
is a square mod
p^{n}
. As a result, the action of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{*}
preserves the set of connected components of
X_{Z,n}
. Then the equality (
2.19
) follows from the fact that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)_{*}[\mathcal{C}]=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d^{-1}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{*}[\mathcal{C}]=[\mathcal{C}]\,,

where the last equality is because the fundamental cycle is compatible with (flat) base change and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d^{-1}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{*}
is an isomorphism (in particular, its degree equals
1
). This concludes the proof.
∎

3.
Diagonal cycles for anticyclotomic twists of Rankin–Selberg products

We keep the definitions in §
2
. Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D<0
and let
\varepsilon_{K}
be the corresponding quadratic character. Assume that
p
splits in
K
as
(p)=\mathfrak{p}\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
. Let
\mathfrak{p}
be the prime of
K
above
p
induced by our fixed embedding
\iota_{p}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Assume also that
p
does not divide the class number of
K
. Building upon the construction of the classes
{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)
in §
2
, we define a
p
-adic family of cohomology classes
z_{n,m}
over ring class fields of
K
with coefficients in a conjugate self-dual twist of
T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}
and prove that they satisfy Euler-system tame norm relations and
Q
-system relations.

3.1.
CM Hida families

Let
\psi
be a Hecke character of
K
of conductor
\mathfrak{c}
coprime to
p
and infinity type
(1-k,0)
for some even integer
k\geq 2
. Let
\chi_{\psi}
be the unique Dirichlet character modulo
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{c})
such that

\psi((n))=n^{k-1}\chi_{\psi}(n)

for integers
n
coprime to
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{c})
. Put
N_{\psi}=N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{c})D
, and let
\theta_{\psi}\in S_{k}(N_{\psi},\chi_{\psi}\varepsilon_{K})
be the theta series attached to
\psi
, i.e.
\theta_{\psi}:=\sum_{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{c})=1}\psi(\mathfrak{a})q^{N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})}
.

3.1.1.

Let
L
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
containing (the image under
\iota_{p}
of)
K
, the Fourier coefficients of
f
and
g
, the roots of the Hecke polynomials of
f
and
g
at
p
, and the image of the Hecke character
\psi
. We denote by
\mathcal{O}
the ring of integers of
L
and by
\mathfrak{P}
its maximal ideal. Let
\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}
be the
p
-adic avatar of
\psi
, i.e. the continuous
L
-valued character of
K^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{K,f}^{\times}
defined by

\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}(x)=x_{\mathfrak{p}}^{1-k}\psi(x).

We also denote by
\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}
the corresponding character of
G_{K}
obtained via the (geometrically normalised) Artin map. The
p
-adic representation attached to the eigenform
\theta_{\psi}
is given by
\mathrm{Ind}_{K}^{\mathbb{Q}}L(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}})
. Since
(\mathfrak{c},p)=1
and
k
is even, it easily follows that the corresponding residual representation is absolutely irreducible and
p
-distinguished (see
[
32
, Prop. 5.1.2]
and
[
32
, Remark 5.1.3]
).

3.1.2.

Let
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the Galois group of the unique
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
unramified outside
\mathfrak{p}
. We can identify
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathfrak{p}}^{(1)}\cong 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
via the Artin map. Let
\lambda
denote the unique Hecke character of infinity type
(-1,0)
and conductor
\mathfrak{p}
whose
p
-adic avatar
\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}}:K^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{K,f}^{\times}\rightarrow L^{\times}
, defined by

\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}}(x)=x_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\lambda(x),

factors through
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Under the identification
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}\cong 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, the map
\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}}
is given by
u\mapsto u^{-1}
. We write
\psi=\psi_{0}\lambda^{k-2}
. Then
\psi_{0}
is a Hecke character of
K
of conductor dividing
\mathfrak{cp}
and infinity-type
(-1,0)
. We denote by
\psi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}
its
p
-adic avatar.

3.1.3.

Let
\Lambda=\mathcal{O}\llbracket 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rrbracket
. Let
\bm{\kappa}:1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
be the tautological character given by
u\mapsto[u]
. Let
\bm{\kappa}^{1/2}:1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
denote the unique square root of
\bm{\kappa}
. Let
\bm{\lambda}:\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
be the character defined by
\sigma\mapsto[\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}}(\sigma)]
(i.e.
\bm{\lambda}=\bm{\kappa}\circ\lambda_{\mathfrak{P}}
). We use the same notation for the corresponding character of
G_{K}
, as well as for the character of
K^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{K,f}
obtained by composition with the Artin map. Consider the
q
-expansion

\textup{\bf h}=\sum_{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{cp})=1}\psi_{0}(\mathfrak{a})\bm{\lambda}(\mathfrak{a})q^{N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})}\in\Lambda\llbracket q\rrbracket.

Then
h
is a primitive Hida family of tame level
N_{\psi}
and character
\chi_{\psi}\varepsilon_{K}\omega^{k-2}
passing through the ordinary
p
-stabilization of
\theta_{\psi}
. The big Galois representation attached to
h
is given by
\mathrm{Ind}_{K}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Lambda(\psi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}\bm{\lambda})
.

3.2.
Jacquet–Langlands correspondence for CM forms

Recall that
B
denotes an indefinite quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
of discriminant
N^{-}
. The main goal of this subsection is to give conditions under which the eigenform
\theta_{\psi}
introduced above can be seen as the Jacquet–Langlands lift of an eigenform
\varphi
on
B^{\times}
.

Let
\pi=\otimes^{\prime}_{v}\pi_{v}
be the automorphic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
defined by
\theta_{\psi}
. Since
\theta_{\psi}
is the theta series attached to the Hecke character
\psi
, the automorphic representation
\pi
is the automorphic induction of
\psi
. In particular, we have the following description of its local constituents:

(i)

At finite places
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
which split in
K
, say
v=w\overline{w}
, the local representation
\pi_{v}
is the principal series
\pi(\psi_{w},\psi_{\overline{w}})
.

(ii)

At finite places
v
of
\mathbb{Q}
which do not split in
K
, the local representation
\pi_{v}
is the one corresponding to the Langlands parameter
\mathrm{Ind}_{W_{K_{v}}}^{W_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}}\psi_{v}
. Let
c
denote the non-trivial element of
\operatorname{Gal}(K_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v})
and let
\psi_{v}^{c}=\psi_{v}\circ c
. We consider the following two subcases:

(a)

Suppose that
\psi_{v}\neq\psi_{v}^{c}
. Then the representation
\mathrm{Ind}_{W_{K_{v}}}^{W_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}}\psi_{v}
is irreducible and therefore
\pi_{v}
is supercuspidal.

(b)

Suppose that
\psi_{v}=\psi_{v}^{c}
. This is equivalent to assuming that there exists a character
\mu_{v}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{v}^{\times}
such that
\psi_{v}=\mu_{v}\circ N_{K_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
. Let
\omega_{K_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
denote the quadratic character of
\mathbb{Q}_{v}^{\times}
attached to the extension
K_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}
. Then
\mathrm{Ind}_{W_{K_{v}}}^{W_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}}\psi_{v}=\mu_{v}\oplus\omega_{K_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\mu_{v}
and
\pi_{v}
is the principal series
\pi(\mu_{v},\omega_{K_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{v}}\mu_{v})
.

(iii)

At the infinite place
\infty
of
\mathbb{Q}
, the local representation
\pi_{\infty}
is the holomorphic discrete series
\sigma_{k}
.

In case (ii), note that if
\psi_{v}
is unramified, then
\psi_{v}=\psi_{v}^{c}
, so we are in subcase (ii)(b).

Let
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{c})=N_{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}N_{\mathfrak{c}}^{-}
, where all primes dividing
N_{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}
split in
K
and all primes dividing
N_{\mathfrak{c}}^{-}
do not split in
K
. It is known that an eigenform
f
on
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
is the Jacquet–Langlands lift of an eigenform
\varphi
on
B^{\times}
if and only if for all places
v\mid N^{-}
the corresponding local representation is a discrete series. Therefore, based on the previous discussion, we have the following result.

Proposition 3.1
.

The eigenform
\theta_{\psi}
is the Jacquet–Langlands lift of an eigenform
\varphi
on
B^{\times}
if and only if the following condition holds:

\mathbf{JL}_{\rm CM}
)

For all places
v\mid N^{-}
, we have
v\mid N_{\mathfrak{c}}^{-}
and
\psi_{v}\neq\psi_{v}^{c}
.

Assume that (

JL
CM

)
) holds. Let
N=N^{+}N^{-}
be a positive integer as in §
2.1
and assume that
N_{\psi}\mid N
. For each natural number
n
and for each positive integer
m
coprime to
Np
, we put

U_{n}:=\{g\in U_{1}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\star\\
0&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\,,\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}\,,

U^{m}:=\{g\in U_{1}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{q}(g_{q})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\star&\star\\
0&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }m^{2}\mathbb{Z}_{q})\text{ for }q|m\}\,,

U_{n}^{m}:=U_{n}\cap U^{m}\,.

We denote by
X(U_{n}^{m})
the Shimura curve of level
U_{n}^{m}
, and we denote by
T_{B}(U_{n}^{m})
the algebra of Hecke correspondences of level
U_{n}^{m}
generated by the operators
\{\mathrm{T}_{\ell}^{\prime},\mathrm{S}_{\ell},\mathrm{S}_{\ell}^{-1}\,|\,\ell\nmid Np\}
and
\{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},\langle x\rangle\,|\,x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}
. We define

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(X(U_{\infty}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O}):=\varprojlim_{n}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O}).

Let
\mathbb{T}^{\mathrm{a.o.}}_{B,\mathcal{O}}(U_{\infty}^{m})
denote the subalgebra of
{\rm End}_{\mathcal{O}}(e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(X(U_{\infty}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O}))
generated by the Hecke operators
\{\mathrm{T}_{\ell}^{\prime},\mathrm{S}_{\ell},\mathrm{S}_{\ell}^{-1}\,|\,\ell\neq p\}
and
\{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime},\langle x\rangle\,|\,x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}
.

In the next subsection, following
[
32
]
, we carry out a patching argument to obtain a family of cohomology classes satisfying suitable norm relations. However, the results of
[
32
]
rely on the following crucial input (stated here for the levels that will be relevant in our case).

Theorem 3.2
.

Assume that
B=\mathrm{M}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. Let
\mathfrak{m}
be a non-Eisenstein
p
-distinguished maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{\mathrm{a.o.}}_{B,\mathcal{O}}(U_{\infty}^{m})
. Then
e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(X(U_{\infty}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})
is a free
\mathbb{T}^{\mathrm{a.o.}}_{B,\mathcal{O}}(U_{\infty}^{m})
-module of rank
2
.

Proof.

With a different choice of tower of level structures at
p
, this follows from
[
19
, Prop. 3.3.1]
. The comparison of the relevant cohomology groups for the choice of level structures at
p
in
op. cit.
and the choice in the present article is explained in Appendix
A
.
∎

At present, we are not aware of an analogous result for arbitrary Shimura curves over
\mathbb{Q}
in the level of generality that we need. Therefore, in the rest of this article we assume that
B=\mathrm{M}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
.

3.3.
Patching

We keep the definitions and notations in the previous subsections and consider the Hecke character
\varphi=\psi|\cdot|^{k/2-1}
of
K
, which has conductor
\mathfrak{c}
and infinity-type
(-k/2,k/2-1)
. We denote by
\varphi_{\mathfrak{P}}
the
p
-adic avatar of
\varphi
.

Let
\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}
be the Galois group of the anticyclotomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
. We can identify
\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}
with the anti-diagonal in
(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cong\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathfrak{p}}^{(1)}\times\mathcal{O}_{K,\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}^{(1)}
via the Artin map. Let

\kappa_{\textup{ac}}:\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

be the character defined by mapping the element
((1+p)^{-1},(1+p))
to
1+p
and let
\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}:\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
be the character defined by mapping the element
((1+p)^{-1},(1+p))
to the group-like element
[1+p]
. We use the same notation for the corresponding characters of
G_{K}
.

Write
\varphi_{\mathfrak{P}}=\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}\kappa_{\textup{ac}}^{2-k}
. Then
\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}
is the
p
-adic avatar of a Hecke character
\varphi_{0}
of
K
of conductor dividing
\mathfrak{cp}
, infinity type
(-1,0)
and central character
\chi_{\varphi_{0}}=\chi_{\psi}\omega^{k-2}
.

For each positive integer
m
, let
K[m]
denote the maximal
p
-subextension of the ring class field of
K
of conductor
m
and let
R_{m}=\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)
. For each positive integer
n
, let
\Gamma_{n}=(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{p^{n-1}}
and
\Lambda_{n}=\mathcal{O}[\Gamma_{n}]
. Note that, via the isomorphism
\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}\cong 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
defined by
\kappa_{\textup{ac}}
, we have the identifications
\Gamma_{n}\cong\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}/\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}^{p^{n-1}}\cong R_{p^{n}}
.

Proposition 3.3
.

There exists a homomorphism
\phi^{m}_{n}:T_{B}(U_{n}^{m})\rightarrow\Lambda_{n}[R_{m}]
defined on generators as follows:

•

For every rational prime
q\nmid Np
,

\phi^{m}_{n}(\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime})=\sum_{\mathfrak{q}}\varphi_{0}(\mathfrak{q})\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}(\mathfrak{q})[\mathfrak{q}],

where the sum runs over ideals of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
coprime to
m\mathfrak{c}
of norm
q
and
[\mathfrak{q}]
denotes the image of
\mathfrak{q}
in
R_{m}
by the Artin map.

•

For every rational prime
q\nmid Np
,

\phi_{n}^{m}(\mathrm{S}_{q}^{-1})=(\chi_{\psi}\epsilon_{K})(q).

•

\phi_{n}^{m}(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})=\psi(\overline{\mathfrak{p}})[\overline{\mathfrak{p}}]
, where
[\overline{\mathfrak{p}}]
denotes the image of
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
in
R_{m}
by the Artin map.

•

for all
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
,

\phi_{n}^{m}(\langle x\rangle)=\omega(x)^{1-k/2}\bm{\kappa}^{-1/2}(x).

Proof.

This follows as in
[
32
, Prop. 3.2.1]
.
∎

Let
m
be a positive integer coprime to
Np
, let
q
be a rational prime coprime to
Np
and let
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Let
\varpi_{11,\ast}
,
\varpi_{12,\ast}
and
\varpi_{22,\ast}
denote the degeneracy maps

\varpi_{ij,\ast}=\varpi_{i,q,\ast}\circ\varpi_{j,q,\ast}:H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{mq})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O}).

Following
[
32
, § 3.3]
, we define norm maps

\mathcal{N}_{m,n}^{mq,n}:\Lambda_{n}[R_{mq}]\otimes_{\phi_{n}^{mq}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{mq})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})\longrightarrow\Lambda_{n}[R_{m}]\otimes_{\phi_{n}^{m}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})

by the following formulae:

•

if
q\mid m
,
\mathcal{N}_{m,n}^{mq,n}=1\otimes\varpi_{11,\ast}
.

•

if
q\nmid m
and
q
splits in
K
as
(q)=\mathfrak{q}\overline{\mathfrak{q}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{m,n}^{mq,n}

\displaystyle=1\otimes\varpi_{11,\ast}-\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\mathfrak{q})\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}(\mathfrak{q})}{q}[\mathfrak{q}]+\frac{\varphi_{0}(\overline{\mathfrak{q}})\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}(\overline{\mathfrak{q}})}{q}[\overline{\mathfrak{q}}]\right)\otimes\varpi_{12,\ast}+\frac{\chi_{\psi}(q)}{q}\otimes\varpi_{22,\ast}\,.

•

if
q\nmid n
and
q
is inert in
K
,

\mathcal{N}_{m,n}^{mq,n}=1\otimes\varpi_{11,\ast}-\frac{\chi_{\psi}(q)}{q}\otimes\varpi_{22,\ast}\,.

Also, for any positive integer
m
coprime to
Np
and for integers
n^{\prime}\geq n\geq 1
, we define the norm map

\mathcal{N}_{m,n}^{m,n^{\prime}}:\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}[R_{m}]\otimes_{\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{m}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n^{\prime}}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})\longrightarrow\Lambda_{n}[R_{m}]\otimes_{\phi_{n}^{m}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})

by
\mathcal{N}_{m,n}^{m,n^{\prime}}=1\otimes\pi_{1,\ast}
. As in
[
32
, Prop. 3.3.2]
, it can be verified that all the norm maps above are well-defined. More generally, given positive integers
m\mid m^{\prime}
coprime to
Np
and integers
n^{\prime}\geq n\geq 1
, we define

\mathcal{N}_{m,n}^{m^{\prime},n^{\prime}}:\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}[R_{m^{\prime}}]\otimes_{\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})\longrightarrow\Lambda_{n}[R_{m}]\otimes_{\phi_{n}^{m}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})

by composing the previously defined norm maps in the natural way.

Proposition 3.4
.

There exists a family of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant homomorphisms of
\Lambda_{n}[R_{m}]
-modules

\nu_{n}^{m}:\Lambda_{n}[R_{m}]\otimes_{\phi_{n}^{m}}e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{K[m]}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Lambda_{n}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}),

for positive integers
m
coprime to
Np
and for positive integers
n
, such that for integers
m\mid m^{\prime}
coprime to
Np
and for integers
n^{\prime}\geq n\geq 1
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}[R_{m^{\prime}}]\otimes_{\phi_{n^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}}e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ind}_{K[m^{\prime}]}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{n}[R_{m}]\otimes_{\phi_{n}^{m}}e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ind}_{K[m]}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Lambda_{n}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{n^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{m,n}^{m^{\prime},n^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Norm}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{n}^{m}}

commutes.

Proof.

This follows from
[
32
, Cor. 5.2.6]
.
∎

3.4.
Norm relations

For the remaining of this section, we assume that the self-duality condition (
(SD)
) holds. Let
r
be a positive integer coprime to
Np
. For
\bullet\in\{1,Z,\emptyset,{\rm spl}\}
and for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we put

U_{\bullet,n}(r)=U_{\bullet,n}\cap U_{0}(r)\subseteq\widehat{B}^{\times}.

Similarly, for
\bullet\in\{1,Z,\emptyset,{\rm spl}\}
and for
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{3}
, we put

\mathcal{U}_{\bullet,\mathbf{n}}(r)=\mathcal{U}_{\bullet,\mathbf{n}}\cap\mathcal{U}_{0}(r)\subseteq\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}

and we define

\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)=X(U_{n_{1}}(r))\times X(U_{n_{2}}(r))\times X(U_{n_{3}}(r))

for each positive integer
r
coprime to
Np
.

Recall that in §
2
we constructed cycles
\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}\in\mathrm{CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}})
. Using the same recipe, one can also construct

{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ}\in\mathrm{CH}^{2}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)).

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
r
be a positive integer coprime to
Np
and let
q
be a rational prime coprime to
Np
. Then, for every
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{3}
,

\displaystyle(\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=(\mathrm{T}_{q},1,1){\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ};

\displaystyle(\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=(1,\mathrm{T}_{q},1){\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ};

\displaystyle(\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{2,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=(1,1,\mathrm{T}_{q}){\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ};

\displaystyle(\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{2,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=(\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime},1,1){\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ};

\displaystyle(\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{2,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=(1,\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime},1){\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ};

\displaystyle(\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=(1,1,\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime}){\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ}.

If
q
is coprime to
r
we have

\displaystyle(\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=(q+1){\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ};

\displaystyle(\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{2,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=(q+1){\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ};

otherwise, if
q\mid r
, we have

\displaystyle(\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=q{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ};

\displaystyle(\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{2,q})_{\ast}\,{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=q{\Delta}_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ}.

Proof.

Let
n=\max\{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\}
. We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{X(U_{n}(rq))\times X(U_{n}(rq))\times X(U_{n}(rq))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1,\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})}
\textstyle{X_{Z,n}(rq)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi_{1,q}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{b}_{(n)}\circ\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})\circ\iota_{n}^{u}}
\textstyle{X(U_{n}(rq))\times X(U_{n}(r))\times X(U_{n}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\varpi_{1,q},1,1)}
\textstyle{X_{Z,n}(r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{b}_{(n)}\circ\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})\circ\iota_{n}^{u}}
\textstyle{X(U_{n}(r))\times X(U_{n}(r))\times X(U_{n}(r))\,.}

Since the horizontal arrows are closed embeddings and the two bottom vertical arrows are finite morphisms of the same degree, it follows that the bottom square is Cartesian.
Let
\bm{1}_{n,rq}\in{\rm CH}^{0}(X_{Z,n}(rq))
denote the fundamental cycle of
X_{Z,n}(rq)
and let
\bm{1}_{n,r}\in{\rm CH}^{0}(X_{Z,n}(r))
denote the fundamental cycle of
X_{Z,n}(r)
. Then
\Delta_{(n)}(rq)^{\circ}=\mathrm{b}_{(n),\ast}\circ\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}\circ\iota_{n,\ast}^{u}(\bm{1}_{n,rq})
and
\Delta_{(n)}(r)^{\circ}=\mathrm{b}_{(n),\ast}\circ\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\ast}\circ\iota_{n,\ast}^{u}(\bm{1}_{n,r})
. By the Cartesianness of the bottom square in the previous diagram, we have

(3.1)

(1,\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,\Delta_{(n)}(rq)^{\circ}=(\varpi_{1,q},1,1)^{\ast}\,\Delta_{(n)}(r)^{\circ}.

Applying
(\varpi_{2,q},1,1)_{\ast}
to both sides of (
3.1
), we obtain

(\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,\Delta_{(n)}(rq)^{\circ}=(\mathrm{T}_{q},1,1)\Delta_{(n)}(r)^{\circ},

whereas applying
(\varpi_{1,q},1,1)_{\ast}
to both sides of (
3.1
), we obtain

(\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,\Delta_{(n)}(rq)^{\circ}=\deg(\varpi_{1,q})\Delta_{(n)}(r)^{\circ}.

Note that
\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbf{n}}^{(n)}\Delta_{(n)}(rq)^{\circ}
and
\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ}=\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbf{n}}^{(n)}\Delta_{(n)}(r)^{\circ}
. Since

\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbf{n}}^{(n)}\circ(\varpi_{i,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}=(\varpi_{i,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\circ\mathrm{pr}_{\mathbf{n}}^{(n)}

for
i\in\{1,2\}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle(\varpi_{2,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{T}_{q},1,1)\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ}\,,\qquad(\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\deg(\varpi_{1,q})\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ}\,.

By the second equality, we have

(\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=q\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ}\,,\qquad\hbox{if $q\mid r$ }

(\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q},\varpi_{1,q})_{\ast}\,\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(rq)^{\circ}=(q+1)\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(r)^{\circ}\,,\qquad\hbox{if $q\nmid r$.}

The remaining identities in the statement can be proved in a similar way.
∎

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
. For
i=1,2
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, consider the degeneracy maps

\varpi_{i,m^{2}}=\prod_{q\mid m}\varpi_{i,q}^{2}:X(U_{n}^{m})\rightarrow X(U_{n}),

where the product is taken over all primes dividing
m
. We then define the cycles

\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(m)=(\varpi_{1,m^{2}},\varpi_{2,m^{2}},1)_{\ast}\,\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(m^{2})^{\circ}\in\mathrm{CH}^{2}(X(U_{n_{1}})\times X(U_{n_{2}})\times X(U_{n_{3}}^{m})).

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
, and let
q
be a rational prime coprime to
Nmp
. Then, for every
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{3}
,

\displaystyle(1,1,\varpi_{11,q})_{\ast}\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(mq)

\displaystyle=\left\{(1,\mathrm{S}_{q}^{2}(\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime})^{2},1)-(q+1)(1,\mathrm{S}_{q},1)\right\}\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(m),

\displaystyle(1,1,\varpi_{12,q})_{\ast}\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(mq)

\displaystyle=\left\{(\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime},\mathrm{S}_{q}\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime},1)-(\mathrm{S}_{q}^{-1},1,\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime})\right\}\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(m),

\displaystyle(1,1,\varpi_{22,q})_{\ast}\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(mq)

\displaystyle=\left\{((\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime})^{2},1,1)-(q+1)(\mathrm{S}_{q}^{-1},1,1)\right\}\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(m).

Proof.

In view of Lemma
3.5
, the argument is identical to that of
[
2
, Lem. 4.4]
.
∎

For each positive integer
n
and for each square-free positive integer
m
coprime to
Np
, we set

H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{1,0,n}^{m}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{f\,g\,\circ}:=T_{f}^{*}\otimes T_{g}^{*}\otimes e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n}^{m})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})\,.

Following the construction in §
2
, we use the cycles
\Delta_{\mathbf{n}}(m)
to define cohomology classes

\Delta_{n,m}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g),\,\widetilde{\Delta}_{n,m}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(f,g)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},H^{3}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(\mathbf{X}_{1,0,n}^{m}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{f\,g\,\circ}(2))\,.

Applying the homomorphisms
\nu_{n}^{m}
of Lemma
3.4
to these classes, we obtain classes

z_{n,m},\,\tilde{z}_{n,m}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{f}^{*}\otimes T_{g}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{Ind}_{K[m]}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Lambda_{n}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})(2))\,.

By Shapiro’s isomorphism, these classes can also be regarded as classes in

H^{1}(K[m],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})(2))\simeq H^{1}(K[mp^{n}],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2))\,.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
n
be a positive integer, let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
and let
q
be a prime coprime to
Nmp
and split in
K
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{cor}_{K[mqp^{n}]/K[mp^{n}]}(z_{n,mq})

\displaystyle=\chi_{g}^{-1}(q)\Bigg\{q\chi_{f}(q)\chi_{g}(q)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\mathfrak{q})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)^{2}-a_{q}(f)a_{q}(g)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\mathfrak{q})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{\chi_{f}^{-1}(q)a_{q}(f)^{2}}{q}+\chi_{g}^{-1}(q)a_{q}(g)^{2}-\frac{q^{2}+1}{q}

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt-a_{q}(f)a_{q}(g)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\overline{\mathfrak{q}})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)+q\chi_{f}(q)\chi_{g}(q)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\overline{\mathfrak{q}})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{2}\Bigg\}z_{n,m},

where
{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}
denotes a geometric Frobenius at
\mathfrak{q}
.

Proof.

The corestriction map in Galois cohomology corresponds to the norm map in Lemma
3.4
under Shapiro’s isomorphism. Hence, the result follows from Lemma
3.4
and Lemma
3.6
after straightforward computations.
∎

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
n
be a positive integer and let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
; then

\mathrm{cor}_{K[mp^{n+1}]/K[mp^{n}]}(z_{n+1,m})=a_{p}(g)z_{n,m}-\chi_{g}(p)\tilde{z}_{n,m}\,,

\mathrm{cor}_{K[mp^{n+1}]/K[mp^{n}]}(\tilde{z}_{n+1,m})=p\cdot z_{n,m}\,.

Proof.

This follows from Corollary
2.16
.
∎

We will henceforth work under the following assumption. We note that this hypothesis will be replaced by its stronger version (
(

BI
0

)
) in §
5.3.5
.

Assumption 3.9
.

The residual
G_{K}
-representation
\overline{T}_{f}^{\ast}\otimes\overline{T}_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2)
satisfies
H^{0}(K,\overline{T}_{f}^{\ast}\otimes\overline{T}_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2))=0
.

Remark 3.10
.

As a consequence of (
(H0)
), we have for any finite
p
-extension
\mathcal{K}
of
K
that

H^{0}(\mathcal{K},\overline{T}_{f}^{\ast}\otimes\overline{T}_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2))=0.

Proposition 3.11
.

For all positive integers
n
and for all square-free products
m
of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
, we have

\tilde{z}_{n+1,m}=\mathrm{res}_{K[mp^{n}]/K[mp^{n+1}]}(z_{n,m}).

Proof.

First we prove that

\tilde{z}_{n+1,m}\in\mathrm{im}\left(H^{1}(K[mp^{n}],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2))\rightarrow H^{1}(K[mp^{n+1}],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2))\right).

By (
(H0)
) and Remark
3.10
, this is equivalent to showing that

\tilde{z}_{n+1,m}\in H^{1}(K[mp^{n+1}],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2))^{G_{K[mp^{n}]}},

which holds by Proposition
2.17
. We therefore know that
\tilde{z}_{n+1,m}=\mathrm{res}_{K[mp^{n}]/K[mp^{n+1}]}(c)
for some

c\in H^{1}(K[mp^{n}],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2)).

Using the second equation in Proposition
3.8
and noting that the composition

\mathrm{cor}_{K[mp^{n+1}]/K[mp^{n}]}\circ\mathrm{res}_{K[mp^{n}]/K[mp^{n+1}]},

equals multiplication by
p
, we deduce that

p\cdot z_{n,m}=\mathrm{cor}_{K[mp^{n+1}]/K[mp^{n}]}(\tilde{z}_{n+1,m})=p\cdot c.

Finally, again by (
(H0)
) and Remark
3.10
, we infer that
H^{1}(K[mp^{n+1}],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2))
is torsion-free, so we obtain
z_{n,m}=c
and therefore
\tilde{z}_{n+1,m}=\mathrm{res}_{K[mp^{n}]/K[mp^{n+1}]}(z_{n,m})
, as required.
∎

Proposition 3.12
.

Let
n\geq 2
be an integer and let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. Then

\mathrm{cor}_{K[mp^{n+1}]/K[mp^{n}]}(z_{n+1,m})=a_{p}(g)z_{n,m}-\chi_{g}(p)\mathrm{res}_{K[mp^{n-1}]/K[mp^{n}]}(z_{n-1,m}).

Proof.

This follows by combining the first equation in Proposition
3.8
and Proposition
3.11
.
∎

Remark 3.13
.

Note that the previous proposition may also be proved using the more conceptual strategy in the proof of Proposition
B.12
.

4.
Iwasawa cohomology classes

As in §
3
,
K
denotes an imaginary quadratic field in which
p
splits. Let
\psi
be a Hecke character of
K
of conductor
\mathfrak{c}
coprime to
p
and infinity type
(1-k,0)
for some even integer
k\geq 2
. Throughout this section,
m
denotes a square-free product of primes that are coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. Furthermore, we assume that (
(H0)
) holds. We explain how the collection
\left\{z_{n,m}\right\}_{n}
gives rise to classes that take values in tempered distributions and bounded signed classes.

4.1.
Classes with values in tempered distributions

We first recall definitions of distributions and the associated norms.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
0\leq\lambda<1
be a real number.

We define the Banach space
\mathcal{C}_{\lambda}
of
L
-valued order-
\lambda
functions on
1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
as in
[
15
, §I.5]
, after identifying the multiplicative group
1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with the additive group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

We let
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
denote the continuous
L
-dual of
\mathcal{C}_{\lambda}
.

Given
\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
, we define the norm

||\mu||_{\lambda}=\sup_{n\geq 0}\,\,\sup_{a\in 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}}p^{-\lfloor\lambda n\rfloor}\left|\left|\int_{a(1+p^{n+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\mu\right|\right|.

Let
\mathscr{L}
denote the
L
-dual of the space of locally constant
L
-valued functions on
1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

Via the Amice transform, we may consider elements of
\mathscr{L}
as power series in
L\llbracket X\rrbracket
that converge on the open unit disk that are
O(\log)
. Those in
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
correspond to the power series that are
O(\log^{\lambda})
. In particular, there is a natural injection
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{L}
for any
0\leq\lambda<1
.

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer. Recall that
\Gamma_{n}=(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{p^{n-1}}=(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We write
\mathscr{L}_{n}
for the dual of the set of functions on
1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
that are spanned by the indicator functions of the cosets in
\Gamma_{n}
. By an abuse of notation, we again write
||\cdot||
for the sup-norm on
\mathscr{L}_{n}
. There is a natural projection
\mathscr{L}_{n^{\prime}}\to\mathscr{L}_{n}
for all
n^{\prime}\geq n
. When viewed as power series, this is the map given by the projection modulo
(1+X)^{p^{n-1}}-1
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
0\leq\lambda<1
be a real number. Let
(\mu_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of elements in
\mathscr{L}_{n}
such that for all
n\geq 1
, we have

a)

||p^{\lambda(n-1)}\mu_{n}||\leq 1
;

b)

the image of
\mu_{n+1}
in
\mathscr{L}_{n}
is
\mu_{n}
.

Then there exists a unique
\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
such that the image of
\mu
in
\mathscr{L}_{n}
is
\mu_{n}
for all
n
, and
||\mu||_{\lambda}\leq 1
.

Proof.

This is a special case of
[
10
, Lemma 2.2]
.
∎

Recall that
z_{n,m}
belongs to

H^{1}(K[m],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})(2))\simeq H^{1}(K[mp^{n}],T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2))\,.

For notational simplicity, we write
M
for the
G_{K[m]}
-representation
T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2)
. We shall identify
z_{n,m}
with its image under the natural map

H^{1}(K[mp],M\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\to H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n}).

We write
||\cdot||_{M}
for the natural norm on
M
induced by the
\mathcal{O}
-module structure of
M
and the
p
-adic norm on
\mathcal{O}
. For
G\subseteq G_{K[m]}
, the continuous cohomology group
H^{1}(G,M\otimes\mathscr{L})
is endowed with a supremum semi-norm, which we still denote by
||\cdot||_{M}
. We use the same notation for the semi-norm on
H^{1}(G,M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n})
.

The following proposition is a slight alteration of a special case of
[
35
, Proposition 2.3.2]
.

Proposition 4.3
.

For all real numbers
\lambda
such that
0\leq\lambda<1
, there is an injection

\Phi_{\lambda}:H^{1}(K[mp^{\infty}],M\otimes\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})\longrightarrow H^{1}(K[mp^{\infty}],M\otimes\mathscr{L}).

Let
\mu\in H^{1}(K[mp^{\infty}],M\otimes\mathscr{L})
, and write
\mu_{n}
for its image in
H^{1}(K[mp^{\infty}],M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n})
induced by the natural map
\mathscr{L}\rightarrow\mathscr{L}_{n}
. Then
\mu
is in the image of
\Phi_{\lambda}
if and only if the sequence

p^{-\lfloor\lambda(n-1)\rfloor}\left|\left|\mu_{n}\right|\right|_{M}

is bounded as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Moreover, if
\left|\left|\mu_{n}\right|\right|_{M}\leq p^{\lambda(n-1)}
for all
n\geq 1
, then
||\mu||_{M,\lambda}\leq 1
,
where
||\cdot||_{M,\lambda}
is the natural semi-norm on
H^{1}(K[mp^{\infty}],M\otimes\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
induced by the norms on
M
and
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

The existence of the morphism
\Phi_{\lambda}
follows from the natural injection
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{L}
.
By
[
35
, Proposition 2.1.2]
, the set of continuous coboundaries
B^{1}(G_{K[mp^{\infty}]},M)
is closed in the set of continuous cocycles
Z^{1}(G_{K[mp^{\infty}]},M)
. Consequently, the injectivity of
\Phi_{\lambda}
follows from
[
14
, proof of Proposition II.2.1]
. The remainder of the proposition follows by adapting the proof of
[
35
, Proposition 2.3.2]
, replacing the use of Lemma 2.2.6 in op. cit. by Lemma
4.2
(which tells us that the constant
C
appearing there can be taken to be
1
in our setting).
∎

For
n\geq 2
, we write

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}^{n}_{n-1}

\displaystyle:H^{1}({K[m]},M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n})\rightarrow H^{1}({K[m]},M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n-1}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}^{n}_{n-1}

\displaystyle:H^{1}({K[m]},M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n-1})\rightarrow H^{1}({K[m]},M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n})

for the natural maps induced by the projection
\mathscr{L}_{n}\rightarrow\mathscr{L}_{n-1}
and the trace map
\mathscr{L}_{n-1}\rightarrow\mathscr{L}_{n}
induced by
\Gamma_{n-1}\ni\sigma\mapsto\displaystyle\sum_{\mathrm{pr}^{n}_{n-1}(\tau)=\sigma}\tau
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
\lambda
be a real number such that
0\leq\lambda<1
. Suppose we are given a sequence of elements
\mu_{n}\in H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n})
,
n\geq 0
such that

i)

For all
n\geq 2
,
\mathrm{pr}^{n}_{n-1}\mu_{n}=\mu_{n-1}
,

ii)

||\mu_{n}||_{M}\leq p^{\lambda(n-1)}
for all
n\geq 1
.

Then there is a unique element
\mu\in H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
whose image in
H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n})
is equal to
\mu_{n}
for all
n
. Furthermore,
||\mu||_{M,\lambda}\leq 1
.

Proof.

Under (
(H0)
), we have
H^{0}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)=0
. Thus, by the inflation-restriction exact sequence and Shapiro’s lemma, we have

H^{1}(K[mp^{\infty}],M\otimes\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})^{\Gamma}\cong H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}),\quad H^{1}(K[mp^{\infty}],M\otimes L)^{\Gamma^{p^{n-1}}}\cong H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n}).

In particular, we can identify
\mu_{n}
as elements of

H^{1}(K[mp^{\infty}],M\otimes L)^{\Gamma^{p^{n-1}}}
. The proposition follows from
[
35
, proof of Proposition 2.3.3]
after replacing Proposition 2.3.2 therein by Proposition
4.3
.
∎

Definition 4.5
.

Let
\alpha
and
\beta
denote the roots of the Hecke polynomial of
g
at
p
.
For
\xi\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
and
n\geq 2
, define

z_{m,n,\xi}=\frac{1}{\xi^{n}}\left(z_{m,n}-\frac{\chi_{g}(p)}{\xi}\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}z_{m,n-1}\right)\,\in\,H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n}).

Theorem 4.6
.

For
\xi\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
with
\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\xi)<1
, there exists a unique element

z_{m,\infty,\xi}\in H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\xi)})

such that its image under the natural morphism
H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathscr{L})\longrightarrow H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n})
is equal to
z_{m,n,\xi}
. Furthermore,
||z_{m,\infty,\xi}||_{M,\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\xi)}\leq|\xi|^{-2}
.

Proof.

It follows from Proposition
3.12
that

\mathrm{pr}_{n-1}^{n}z_{m,n,\xi}=z_{m,n-1,\xi}.

Therefore, the theorem is a direct consequence of Proposition
4.4
.
∎

4.2.
Integral signed classes

We now explain how the classes given by Theorem
4.6
decompose into integral ones under the assumption that
g
is
p
-nonordinary. We first define the logarithmic matrix attached to
g
(which finds its origin in
[
42
]
). The notation we use here is based on the discussion in
[
10
, §2.2]
. We identify
\Lambda
with
\mathcal{O}\llbracket X\rrbracket
. For each
n\geq 1
, let
\omega_{n}=(1+X)^{p^{n}}-1
.

Definition 4.7
.

Let

A_{g}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\frac{-1}{\chi_{g}(p)p}\\
\\
1&\frac{a_{p}(g)}{\chi_{g}(p)p}\end{bmatrix},\quad Q_{g}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&-\beta\\
-\chi_{g}(p)p&\chi_{g}(p)p\end{bmatrix},\quad C_{g,n}=\begin{bmatrix}a_{p}(g)&1\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\Phi_{n}&0\end{bmatrix}

for
n\geq 1
, where
\Phi_{n}=\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}(1+X)^{jp^{n-1}}
denotes the
p^{n}
-th cyclotomic polynomial in
1+X
.

The limit of the sequence
((\alpha-\beta)(A_{g})^{n+1}C_{g,n}\cdots C_{g,1})_{n\geq 1}
is denoted by
M_{\log,g}
.

It follows from
[
10
, Lemma 2.8]
that the entries in the first column of
Q_{g}^{-1}M_{\log,g}
are
O(\log^{\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\alpha)})
, while those in the second column are
O(\log^{\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\beta)})
.

Lemma 4.8
.

For
n\geq 1
, let
\Theta_{g,n}
denote the map

\Lambda^{\oplus 2}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}[\Gamma_{n}]^{\oplus 2},\quad\begin{bmatrix}F\\
G\end{bmatrix}\mapsto C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1}\begin{bmatrix}F\\
G\end{bmatrix}\mod\omega_{n-1}.

Then
\displaystyle\varprojlim\Lambda^{\oplus 2}/\ker\Theta_{g,n}=\Lambda^{\oplus 2}
.

Proof.

See
[
10
, Lemma 2.9]
.
∎

For integers
n^{\prime}\geq n\geq 1
, let

\mathrm{pr}^{n^{\prime}}_{n}:\mathcal{O}[\Gamma_{n^{\prime}}]\to\mathcal{O}[\Gamma_{n}]

denote the natural projection map and write

\operatorname{Tr}^{n^{\prime}}_{n}:\mathcal{O}[\Gamma_{n}]\to\mathcal{O}[\Gamma_{n^{\prime}}]

for the trace map defined by
\Gamma_{n}\ni\sigma\mapsto\displaystyle\sum_{\mathrm{pr}^{n^{\prime}}_{n}(\tau)=\sigma}\tau
. By an abuse of notation, we use the same notation to denote the maps on the cohomological groups

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}^{n^{\prime}}_{n}

\displaystyle:H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\to H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\to H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})).

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
n^{\prime}\geq n\geq 1
. The images of
\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n^{\prime}}:H^{1}\left(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})\right)\to H^{1}\left(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})\right)
and the natural map

H^{1}\left(K[m],M\otimes\left(\omega_{n^{\prime}-1}/\omega_{n-1}\right)\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})\right)\to H^{1}\left(\mathbb{Q},M\otimes\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})\right)

coincide. Furthermore, if
z\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},M\otimes\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))
, then
\omega_{n^{\prime}-1}/\omega_{n-1}\cdot z=\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n^{\prime}}\circ\mathrm{pr}^{n^{\prime}}_{n}(z)
.

Proof.

Let
\gamma
be a topological generator of
\operatorname{Gal}(K[mp^{\infty}]/K[m])
that corresponds to
1+X
under the identification
\mathcal{O}\llbracket\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}\rrbracket=\mathcal{O}\llbracket X\rrbracket
.
By Shapiro’s lemma

H^{1}\left(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda_{r}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})\right)\cong H^{1}\left(K[mp^{r}],M\right),\quad r\in\{n,n^{\prime}\}.

The morphisms
\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n^{\prime}}
and
\mathrm{pr}_{n}^{n^{\prime}}
correspond to corestriction and restriction maps, respectively. The corestriction map can be realized by the action of

\sum_{i=0}^{p^{n^{\prime}-n}-1}\gamma^{ip^{n-1}}=\frac{\gamma^{p^{n^{\prime}-1}}-1}{\gamma^{p^{n-1}}-1}=\omega_{n^{\prime}-1}/\omega_{n-1},

from which the lemma follows. ∎

In what follows,
\mathrm{pr}^{\infty}_{n}:H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\to H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))
denotes the natural projection map.

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
(\kappa_{n})_{n\geq 0}
be a collection of classes such that
\kappa_{n}\in H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))
for all
n
and

(4.1)

\mathrm{pr}^{n}_{n-1}\kappa_{n}=a_{p}(g)\kappa_{n-1}-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-2}^{n-1}\kappa_{n-2},\quad n\geq 3.

Then there exist unique
\kappa_{\sharp},\kappa_{\flat}\in H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))
such that

\begin{bmatrix}\kappa_{n}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}\kappa_{n-1}\end{bmatrix}=C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{pr}^{\infty}_{n}\kappa_{\sharp}\\
\mathrm{pr}^{\infty}_{n}\kappa_{\flat}\end{bmatrix}.

Proof.

Our proof is based on a slight modification of the proof presented in
[
7
, Theorem 3.4]
.
Let
C_{g,i}^{\prime}=\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
\chi_{g}(p)\Phi_{i}&a_{p}(g)\end{bmatrix}
be the adjugate matrix of
C_{g,i}
so that
C_{g,i}C_{g,i}^{\prime}=\det C_{g,i}
. We show inductively that for all
n\geq 2

(4.2)

C_{g,1}^{\prime}\cdots C_{g,n-1}^{\prime}\begin{bmatrix}\kappa_{n}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}\kappa_{n-1}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathrm{Im}\operatorname{Tr}_{1}^{n}.

When
n=2
,

C_{g,1}^{\prime}\begin{bmatrix}\kappa_{2}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{1}^{2}\kappa_{1}\end{bmatrix}=\chi_{g}(p)\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Tr}_{1}^{2}\kappa_{1}\\
\Phi_{1}\kappa_{2}-a_{p}(g)\operatorname{Tr}_{1}^{2}\kappa_{1}\end{bmatrix}.

As
\Phi_{1}=\omega_{1}/\omega_{0}
, Lemma
4.9
says that
\Phi_{1}\kappa_{2}
is in the image of
\operatorname{Tr}^{2}_{1}
. Thus, (
4.2
) holds for
n=2
.

Suppose that (
4.2
) holds for some
n\geq 2
. Then

\displaystyle C_{g,1}^{\prime}\cdots C_{g,n}^{\prime}\begin{bmatrix}\kappa_{n+1}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}\kappa_{n}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\chi_{g}(p)C_{g,1}^{\prime}\cdots C_{g,n-1}^{\prime}\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}\kappa_{n}\\
\Phi_{n}\kappa_{n+1}-a_{p}(g)\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}\kappa_{n}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\chi_{g}(p)C_{g,1}^{\prime}\cdots C_{g,n-1}^{\prime}\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}\kappa_{n}\\
\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}\circ\mathrm{pr}_{n}^{n+1}\kappa_{n+1}-a_{p}(g)\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}\kappa_{n}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\chi_{g}(p)C_{g,1}^{\prime}\cdots C_{g,n-1}^{\prime}\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}\kappa_{n}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}\circ\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}\kappa_{n-1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}\left(C_{g,1}^{\prime}\cdots C_{g,n-1}^{\prime}\begin{bmatrix}\kappa_{n}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}\kappa_{n-1}\end{bmatrix}\right),

where the second equality follows from Lemma
4.9
and the third equality is a consequence of the trace relation given by (
4.1
).
Therefore, by the inductive hypothesis, (
4.2
) holds for
n+1
.

For each
n\geq 0
, we can choose a
M\otimes\Lambda_{n}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})
-valued cocycle on
G_{K[m]}
recursively so that

\mathrm{pr}^{n}_{n-1}c_{n}=a_{p}(g)c_{n-1}-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-2}^{n-1}c_{n-2},\quad n\geq 2.

Indeed, after
c_{n-1}
and
c_{n-2}
are chosen, any representative
c_{n}
of
\kappa_{n}
satisfies

\mathrm{pr}^{n}_{n-1}c_{n}-a_{p}(g)c_{n-1}+\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-2}^{n-1}c_{n-2}=b_{x},

where
b_{x}:\sigma\mapsto\sigma\cdot x-x
is a coboundary defined by some element
x\in M\otimes\Lambda_{n-1}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})
. We can replace
c_{n}
by
c_{n}-b_{x^{\prime}}
, where
b_{x^{\prime}}
is the coboundary defined by any pre-image
x^{\prime}\in M\otimes\Lambda_{n}
of
x
. Then

(4.3)

\mathrm{pr}^{n}_{n-1}c_{n}-a_{p}(g)c_{n-1}+\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-2}^{n-1}c_{n-2}=0,

For any
\sigma\in G_{K[m]}
, (
4.2
) implies that there exist
c_{\sharp,n}(\sigma),c_{\flat,n}(\sigma)\in M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})
such that

\begin{bmatrix}c_{n}(\sigma)\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}c_{n-1}(\sigma)\end{bmatrix}=C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1}\begin{bmatrix}c_{\sharp,n}(\sigma)\\
c_{\flat,n}(\sigma)\end{bmatrix}\mod\omega_{n-1}

since
\det(C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1})=\chi_{g}(p)^{n-1}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\Phi_{i}
. Let
\Theta_{g,n}
denote the map on
\Lambda^{2}_{n}
given as multiplication by
C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1}
as in Lemma
4.8
.
As
c_{n}
and
c_{n-1}
are cocycles on
G_{K[m]}
, the map
\sigma\mapsto(c_{\sharp,n}(\sigma),c_{\flat,n}(\sigma))
defines a
M\otimes\Lambda_{n}^{2}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})/\ker\Theta_{g,n}
-valued cocycle on
G_{K[m]}
.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}c_{n+1}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n}^{n+1}c_{n}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\equiv C_{g,n}C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1}\begin{bmatrix}c_{\sharp,n+1}\\
c_{\flat,n+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\mod\omega_{n}

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}a_{p}(g)c_{n}-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}c_{n-1}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)pc_{n}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{bmatrix}a_{p}(g)&1\\
-\chi_{g}(p)p&0\end{bmatrix}C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{pr}_{n}^{n+1}c_{\sharp,n+1}\\
\mathrm{pr}_{n}^{n+1}c_{\flat,n+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\mod\omega_{n-1}

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}c_{n}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}c_{n-1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\equiv C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{pr}_{n}^{n+1}c_{\sharp,n+1}\\
\mathrm{pr}_{n}^{n+1}c_{\flat,n+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\mod\omega_{n-1},

where the second congruence is a consequence of (
4.3
).
Thus,
(c_{\sharp,n},c_{\flat,n})
form an inverse system of cocycles taking values in

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{n}M\otimes\Lambda_{n}^{2}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})/\ker\Theta_{g,n}=M\otimes\Lambda^{2}(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})
(where the validity of the equality follows from Lemma
4.8
). This concludes the proof.
∎

Theorem 4.11
.

There exist unique
z_{m}^{\sharp},z_{m}^{\flat}\in H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))
such that

\begin{bmatrix}z_{m,\infty,\alpha}\\
z_{m,\infty,\beta}\end{bmatrix}=Q_{g}^{-1}M_{\log,g}\begin{bmatrix}z_{m}^{\sharp}\\
z_{m}^{\flat}\end{bmatrix}.

Furthermore, let
q
be a prime coprime to
Nmp
and split in
K
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{cor}_{K[mq]/K[m]}z_{mq}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=\chi_{g}^{-1}(q)\Bigg\{q\chi_{f}(q)\chi_{g}(q)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\mathfrak{q})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)^{2}-a_{q}(f)a_{q}(g)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\mathfrak{q})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\chi_{f}^{-1}(q)a_{q}(f)^{2}}{q}+\chi_{g}^{-1}(q)a_{q}(g)^{2}-\frac{q^{2}+1}{q}

\displaystyle-a_{q}(f)a_{q}(g)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\overline{\mathfrak{q}})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)+q\chi_{f}(q)\chi_{g}(q)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\overline{\mathfrak{q}})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{2}\Bigg\}z_{m}^{\bullet}

for
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
.

Proof.

Recall that for
\xi\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
, the image of
z_{m,\infty,\xi}
in
H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\mathscr{L}_{n})
is given by

z_{m,n,\xi}=\frac{1}{\xi^{n}}\left(z_{m,n}-\frac{\chi_{g}(p)}{\xi}\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}z_{m,n-1}\right).

As
Q_{g}^{-1}A_{g}Q_{g}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha^{-1}&0\\
0&\beta^{-1}\end{bmatrix}
, we have

\displaystyle A_{g}^{-n}Q_{g}\begin{bmatrix}z_{m,n,\alpha}\\
z_{m,n,\beta}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha&-\beta\\
-\alpha\beta&\alpha\beta\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha^{n}&0\\
0&\beta^{n}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\alpha^{-n}&\alpha^{-n-1}\\
\beta^{-n}&\beta^{-n-1}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}z_{m,n}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}z_{m,n-1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=(\alpha-\beta)\begin{bmatrix}z_{m,n}\\
-\chi_{g}(p)\operatorname{Tr}_{n-1}^{n}z_{m,n-1}\end{bmatrix}.

Therefore, by Proposition
3.12
and Theorem
4.10
, there exist unique classes
z_{m}^{\sharp},z_{m}^{\flat}\in H^{1}(K[m],M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))
such that

A_{g}^{-n}Q_{g}\begin{bmatrix}z_{m,n,\alpha}\\
z_{m,n,\beta}\end{bmatrix}=(\alpha-\beta)C_{g,n-1}\cdots C_{g,1}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{pr}_{n}^{\infty}z_{m}^{\sharp}\\
\mathrm{pr}_{n}^{\infty}z_{m}^{\flat}\end{bmatrix}.

Letting
n\rightarrow\infty
, the first assertion of the theorem follows.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{q}

\displaystyle=\chi_{g}^{-1}(q)\Bigg\{q\chi_{f}(q)\chi_{g}(q)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\mathfrak{q})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)^{2}-a_{q}(f)a_{q}(g)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\mathfrak{q})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\chi_{f}^{-1}(q)a_{q}(f)^{2}}{q}+\chi_{g}^{-1}(q)a_{q}(g)^{2}-\frac{q^{2}+1}{q}

\displaystyle-a_{q}(f)a_{q}(g)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\overline{\mathfrak{q}})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)+q\chi_{f}(q)\chi_{g}(q)\left(\frac{\varphi_{0}(\overline{\mathfrak{q}})}{q}{\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}}\right)^{2}\Bigg\}.

It follows from Proposition
3.7
and the first assertion of the theorem applied to
mq
that

\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{m,\infty,\alpha}\\
\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{m,\infty,\beta}\end{bmatrix}=Q_{g}^{-1}M_{\log,g}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{cor}_{K[mq]/K[m]}z_{mq}^{\sharp}\\
\mathrm{cor}_{K[mq]/K[m]}z_{mq}^{\flat}\end{bmatrix}.

After applying
\mathcal{P}_{q}
to both sides of the equation given by the first assertion of the theorem, we have

\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{m,\infty,\alpha}\\
\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{m,\infty,\beta}\end{bmatrix}=Q_{g}^{-1}M_{\log,g}\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{m}^{\sharp}\\
\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{m}^{\flat}\end{bmatrix}.

Therefore, by the uniqueness of the signed classes,

\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{cor}_{K[mq]/K[m]}z_{mq}^{\sharp}\\
\mathrm{cor}_{K[mq]/K[m]}z_{mq}^{\flat}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{m}^{\sharp}\\
\mathcal{P}_{q}\cdot z_{m}^{\flat}\end{bmatrix},

and the second assertion follows.
∎

5.
Arithmetic applications

In this section, we use the signed classes constructed in §
4.2
to obtain applications towards signed main conjectures. We keep the assumptions and notations introduced in previous sections, and for a technical reason (cf. Remark
5.9
), we restrict our discussion to the case
a_{p}(g)=0
.

5.1.
Coleman maps and Selmer groups

In this subsection, we introduce Coleman maps and use them to define the Selmer groups that we will consider in our applications.

Since
f
is
p
-ordinary, we have an exact sequence of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representations

0\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{+}V_{f}^{\ast}\rightarrow V_{f}^{\ast}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{-}V_{f}^{\ast}\rightarrow 0,

where
\mathscr{F}^{+}V_{f}^{\ast}
and
\mathscr{F}^{-}V_{f}^{\ast}
are one-dimensional with Hodge–Tate weights
0
and
-1
, respectively, with the convention that the Hodge–Tate weight of the cyclotomic character is
+1
. Also,
\mathscr{F}^{+}V_{f}^{\ast}
is unramified with the geometric Frobenius
{\rm Fr}_{p}
acting as multiplication by
\alpha_{f}
(the unit root of the Hecke polynomial of
f
at the prime
p
). We define

\mathscr{F}^{+}T_{f}^{\ast}=T_{f}^{\ast}\cap\mathscr{F}^{+}V_{f}^{\ast}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathscr{F}^{-}T_{f}^{\ast}=T_{f}^{\ast}/\mathscr{F}^{+}T_{f}^{\ast}.

We also define

\mathscr{F}^{+}M=\mathscr{F}^{+}T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathscr{F}^{-}M=\mathscr{F}^{-}T_{f}^{\ast}\otimes T_{g}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c})(2)=M/\mathscr{F}^{+}M.

We remark that the
G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}}
-representation
\mathscr{F}^{+}M|_{G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
and the
G_{K_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-representation
\mathscr{F}^{-}M|_{G_{K_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
both have Hodge–Tate weights
1
and
0
.

Let
(\mathfrak{q},T)\in\{(\overline{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M),(\mathfrak{p},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)\}
. Let
F
be a finite unramified extension of
K_{\mathfrak{q}}
. We write

\mathcal{L}_{T,\mathfrak{q},F}:H^{1}(F,T\otimes\Lambda_{\textup{cyc}}^{\iota})\to\mathcal{H}(\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}})\otimes\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(F,T)

for the Perrin-Riou map, where
\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}}=\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathfrak{q}}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/K_{\mathfrak{q}})
,
\Lambda_{\textup{cyc}}
is the Iwasawa algebra
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}}\rrbracket
,
\iota
denotes the Galois action of
G_{F}
which is the inverse of the one induced by
G_{F}\rightarrow\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}}\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{\textup{cyc}}
and
\mathcal{H}(\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}})
is the algebra of tempered distributions. Following the construction in
[
34
]
, after taking inverse limit of
\mathcal{L}_{T,\mathfrak{q},F_{n}}
, where
F_{n}
is the unramified extension of degree
p^{n}
over
F
, we obtain a “two-variable” Perrin-Riou map:

\mathcal{L}_{T,\mathfrak{q},F_{\infty}}:H^{1}(F,T\otimes\Lambda_{\textup{cyc}}^{\iota}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\to\mathcal{H}(\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}})\hat{\otimes}\Lambda\otimes\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(F,T).

Here, we implicitly fix a basis
\Omega_{F}
of the Yager module, as in
[
10
, §2.3]
, and we identify
\Lambda_{\textup{cyc}}\hat{\otimes}\Lambda
with the Iwasawa algebra
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket\operatorname{Gal}(F_{\infty}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/F)\rrbracket\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}}\times\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}\rrbracket
.

Given a finite extension
\mathcal{K}
of
K
and a place
\lambda
of
K
, we write

H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{\lambda},T):=\bigoplus_{v\mid\lambda}H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v},T).

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
.
We denote by

\mathcal{L}_{T,\mathfrak{q},m}:H^{1}(K[m]_{\mathfrak{q}},T\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)]\otimes\mathcal{H}(\Gamma_{\textup{ac}})\otimes\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(T|_{G_{K_{\mathfrak{q}}}})

the natural map induced by
\bigoplus\mathcal{L}_{T,\mathfrak{q},K[m]_{v,\infty}}
under projection, where
v
runs over the places lying above
\mathfrak{q}
.

Let
v_{0}
be an
\mathcal{O}
-basis of the
\mathcal{O}
-module
\operatorname{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(T_{g}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})
. Let
\alpha
and
\beta
denote the roots of the Hecke polynomial of
g
at
p
. For
\xi\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
, define the
\varphi
-eigenvector

v_{g,\xi}=\varphi(v_{0})-\frac{1}{\xi}v_{0}\in\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(V_{g}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})

with
\varphi
-eigenvalue
1/{\xi^{\prime}}
, where
\xi^{\prime}\in\{\alpha,\beta\}\setminus\{\xi\}
.

Fix an
\mathcal{O}
-basis
w_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}},+}
of the
\mathcal{O}
-module
\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathscr{F}^{+}T_{f}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}),\mathcal{O}(-1))|_{G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}}})
and an
\mathcal{O}
-basis
w_{\mathfrak{p},-}
of the
\mathcal{O}
-module
\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathscr{F}^{-}T_{f}^{\ast}(\varphi_{0,\mathfrak{P}}^{c}),\mathcal{O}(-1))|_{G_{K_{\mathfrak{p}}}})
.
Let
v_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}},+,\xi}=v_{g,\xi}^{c}\otimes w_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}},+}
and
v_{\mathfrak{p},-,\xi}=v_{g,\xi}\otimes w_{\mathfrak{p},-}
.
Then
\{v_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}},+,\alpha},v_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}},+,\beta}\}
is a
\varphi
-eigenbasis of
\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathscr{F}^{+}M,L(1))|_{G_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}}})
and
\{v_{\mathfrak{p},-,\alpha},v_{\mathfrak{p},-,\beta}\}
is a
\varphi
-eigenbasis of
\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathscr{F}^{-}M,L(1))|_{G_{K_{\mathfrak{p}}}})
.
The calculations in
[
10
, §2.3]
show that

(5.1)

\begin{bmatrix}\langle\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}M,\overline{\mathfrak{p}},m}(-),v_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}},+,\alpha}\rangle\\
\langle\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}M,\overline{\mathfrak{p}},m}(-),v_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}},+,\beta}\rangle\end{bmatrix}=Q_{g}^{-1}M_{\log,g}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}M,\overline{\mathfrak{p}},m}^{\sharp}\\
\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}M,\overline{\mathfrak{p}},m}^{\flat}\end{bmatrix}

for certain homomorphisms of
\Lambda[\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)]
-modules

\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}M,\overline{\mathfrak{p}},m}^{\sharp/\flat}:H^{1}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\longrightarrow\Lambda[\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)]

and similarly

(5.2)

\begin{bmatrix}\langle\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(-),v_{\mathfrak{p},-,\alpha}\rangle\\
\langle\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(-),v_{\mathfrak{p},-,\beta}\rangle\end{bmatrix}=Q_{g}^{-1}M_{\log,g}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}^{\sharp}\\
\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}^{\flat}\end{bmatrix}

for certain homomorphisms of
\Lambda[\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)]
-modules

\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}^{\sharp/\flat}:H^{1}(K[m]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\longrightarrow\Lambda[\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)].

We assume that the following hypothesis holds until the end of §
5
.

(
H
{}^{0}_{-}
)

We have
H^{0}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}\overline{M})=0
.

Remark 5.1
.

As a consequence of (
(H

0

-

)
), for any finite
p
-extension
F
of
K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}
we have
H^{0}(F,\mathscr{F}^{-}\overline{M})=0
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. The map

H^{1}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{1}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))

induced by the inclusion
\mathscr{F}^{+}M\hookrightarrow M
is injective.

Proof.

We have an exact sequence

H^{0}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{1}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\rightarrow H^{1}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})),

so it suffices to show that
H^{0}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))=0
. Now, by (
(H

0

-

)
) and Remark
5.1
, we have that
H^{0}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)=0
for all positive integers
n
. Therefore

H^{0}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))=\varprojlim_{n}H^{0}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)=0,

which concludes the proof.
∎

In light of the previous lemma, we can regard the module
H^{1}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))
as a submodule of
H^{1}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))
.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. Let
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
.

The local condition
H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},M)\subseteq H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},M)
is the kernel of the compositum

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},M)\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}^{\bullet}}\Lambda[\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)],

where the first arrow is induced by the canonical projection
M\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{-}M
.

The local condition
H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M)\subseteq H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M)
is the kernel of the map

\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}M,\overline{\mathfrak{p}},m}^{\bullet}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)\longrightarrow\Lambda[\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)].

At primes
\lambda
of
K
not dividing
p
, we will usually work with the following unramifed condition:

H^{1}_{\textup{ur}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\lambda},M)=\ker\left(H^{1}(K[m]_{\lambda},M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\longrightarrow H^{1}(K[m]^{\textup{ur}}_{\lambda},M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\right).

Definition 5.4
.

Let
\Sigma
be a finite set of finite places of
K
including all places dividing
Np
. Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. Let
K[m](\Sigma)
denote the maximal extension of
K[m]
unramified outside the places of
K[m]
lying above
\Sigma
. Let
\bullet,\star\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
. Then we define

\displaystyle H^{1,\Sigma}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)

\displaystyle=H^{1}(K[m](\Sigma)/K[m],M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1})),

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)

\displaystyle=\ker\left(H^{1,\Sigma}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)\longrightarrow\prod_{\lambda\in\Sigma\setminus\{\mathfrak{p},\overline{\mathfrak{p}}\}}\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\lambda},M)}{H^{1}_{\textup{ur}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\lambda},M)}\right)

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\bullet,\star}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)

\displaystyle=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},M)}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},M)}\bigoplus\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M)}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\star}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M)}\right).

5.2.
Verifying the local conditions

The goal of this subsection is to prove that, for
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
,
the classes
z_{m}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)
introduced in Theorem
4.11
belong to the Selmer groups
H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)
.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
n
be a positive integer and let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. Let
v
be a place of
K[mp^{n}]
above
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
. Then

\textup{res}_{v}(z_{n,m})\in H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},\mathscr{F}^{+}M).

Proof.

It suffices to show that the image of
\textup{res}_{v}(z_{n,m})
in
H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
is zero. By
[
37
, Thm. 5.9]
, we have that
\textup{res}_{v}(z_{n,m})\in H^{1}_{g}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},M)
, so the image of
\textup{res}_{v}(z_{n,m})
in
H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
lies in
H^{1}_{g}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
. We will prove that
H^{1}_{g}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)=0
.

Let
V=\mathscr{F}^{-}M\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}L
, which we regard as a
G_{K[mp^{n}]_{v}}
-representation. Since
V
has Hodge–Tate weights
0
and
-1
, we have
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}(V)/\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(V)=0
. By (
(H

0

-

)
) and Remark
5.1
, we have
H^{0}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)=0
. Also, since
0<\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\alpha),\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\beta)<1
, we have that
\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(V^{\vee}(1))^{\varphi=1}=0
, where
\varphi
denotes the crystalline Frobenius. Hence,

\dim H^{1}_{g}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)=\dim\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}(V)/\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(V)+\dim H^{0}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)+\dim\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(V^{\vee}(1))^{\varphi=1}=0.

Therefore the
\mathcal{O}
-module

H^{1}_{g}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)=\ker\left(H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)\longrightarrow H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)/H^{1}_{g}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)\right)

is torsion. Since
H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
is a free
\mathcal{O}
-module by hypothesis (
(H

0

-

)
) and Remark
5.1
, it follows that
H^{1}_{g}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)=0
.
∎

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. Then,

\textup{res}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}(z_{m,\infty,\xi})\in H^{1}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M\otimes\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\xi)})\,,\qquad\xi\in\{\alpha,\beta\}\,,

\textup{res}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}(z_{m}^{\bullet})\in H^{1}(K[m]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))\,,\qquad\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}\,.

Proof.

This follows easily from the previous proposition and the construction of the classes
z_{m,\infty,\xi}
,
z_{m}^{\bullet}
.
∎

In what follows, we assume that the following hypothesis holds:

(
LOC
)

a_{p}(g)=0
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. Suppose that (
(LOC)
) holds. Then, for
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
, we have

\mathrm{Col}^{\bullet}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m}^{\bullet}))=0\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{Col}^{\bullet}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}M,\overline{\mathfrak{p}},m}(\textup{res}_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}}(z_{m}^{\bullet}))=0.

Proof.

We prove the first equality; the second follows by a completely analogous argument.

Since we are assuming that
a_{p}(g)=0
, the matrix
M_{\log,g}
is of the form
\begin{bmatrix}*\log^{-}&*\log^{+}\\
*\log^{-}&*\log^{+}\end{bmatrix}
, where
*
represents a non-zero constant and
\log^{\pm}
are Pollack’s plus and minus logarithms defined in
[
40
]
(see for example
[
9
, Appendix 4]
).

Let
\eta
be a character of order
p^{n}
of
\Gamma_{\textup{ac}}
. Note that

\displaystyle M_{\log,g}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{Col}^{\sharp}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m}^{\sharp}))&\mathrm{Col}^{\sharp}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m}^{\flat}))\\
\mathrm{Col}^{\flat}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m}^{\sharp}))&\mathrm{Col}^{\flat}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m}^{\flat}))\end{bmatrix}M_{\log,g}^{T}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\langle\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m,\infty,\alpha})),v_{\mathfrak{p},-,\alpha}\rangle&\langle\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m,\infty,\beta})),v_{\mathfrak{p},-,\alpha}\rangle\\
\langle\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m,\infty,\alpha})),v_{\mathfrak{p},-,\beta}\rangle&\langle\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m,\infty,\beta})),v_{\mathfrak{p},-,\beta}\rangle\end{bmatrix}.

Since
\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m,n})\in H^{1}_{g}(K[mp^{n}]_{\mathfrak{p}},M)
by
[
37
, Thm. 5.9]
, the above matrix vanishes when evaluated at
\eta
by the interpolation property of the Perrin-Riou map
\mathcal{L}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}
. If
n
is even, we have
\log^{-}(\eta)=0
. In particular,

\log^{+}(\eta)^{2}\mathrm{Col}^{\flat}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m}^{\flat}))(\eta)=0.

But
\log^{+}(\eta)\neq 0
, which implies that
\mathrm{Col}^{\flat}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m}^{\flat}))(\eta)=0
. As this holds for infinitely many
\eta
, we deduce that
\mathrm{Col}^{\flat}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m}^{\flat}))=0
. On considering odd
n
, we deduce by the same argument that
\mathrm{Col}^{\sharp}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}(\textup{res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{m}^{\sharp}))=0
.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. If
(
(LOC)
)
holds, then for
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
, we have that
z_{m}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)
.

Proof.

Let
n
be a positive integer. Let
V
denote the
p
-adic
G_{K[mp^{n}]}
-representation
M\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}L|_{G_{K[mp^{n}]}}
, which is pure of motivic weight
-1
. Let
v
be a finite place of
K[mp^{n}]
not dividing
Np
and let
I_{v}
denote the inertia subgroup of
G_{K[mp^{n}]_{v}}
. By the weight-monodromy conjecture, established for modular curves by Saito
[
41
]
, the eigenvalues of a geometric Frobenius
{\rm Fr}_{v}
acting on
V^{I_{v}}
have complex absolute value
q_{v}^{1/2}
, where
q_{v}
is the cardinality of the residue field at
v
. In particular, it follows that
H^{0}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)=0
. By the same argument we have that
H^{0}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{v}},V)=0
. Therefore

\displaystyle\dim H^{2}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)

\displaystyle=\dim H^{2}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{v}},V^{c})

\displaystyle=\dim H^{2}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{v}},V^{\vee}(1))=\dim H^{0}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{v}},V)=0.

By the Euler–Poicaré characteristic formula, we deduce that

\dim H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)=\dim H^{0}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)+\dim H^{2}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)=0.

Thus, it trivially holds that

\textup{res}_{v}(z_{m,n})\in H^{1}_{f}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},M):=\ker\left(H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},M)\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)}{H^{1}_{\textup{ur}}(K[mp^{n}]_{v},V)}\right).

Hence, for each finite place
\lambda\nmid Np
of
K
, we have that
\textup{res}_{\lambda}(z_{m}^{\bullet})\in H^{1}_{\textup{ur}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\lambda},M)
. Therefore

z_{m}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}(K[mp^{\infty}],M).

Combined with Corollary
5.6
and Proposition
5.7
, this implies that

z_{m}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}],M),

as desired.
∎

Remark 5.9
.

The only reason why our main results require the hypothesis
(
(LOC)
)
is the verification of the local properties of signed diagonal cycles (as in Proposition
5.8
). The more general case can be treated once we compare our diagonal cycles with those of
[
18
,
5
]
, relying on the reciprocity laws therein. To establish this comparison, however, one needs to allow variation in
f
and
g
as well. This will be treated in the PhD thesis (in progress) of Qingshen Lv.

5.3.
Euler systems

In this subsection, we formulate the signed (anticyclotomic) Iwasawa main conjectures for the trito-non-ordinary product
{\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{f})\times{\rm BC}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\pi_{g})\times\psi
, and explain that the work
[
29
]
implies one inclusion of these conjectures.

5.3.1.

We write
V=M\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}L
and
A=V/M
. Let us assume
7
7

7

In our main results towards anticyclotomic main conjectures (cf. Theorem
5.15
below), we will require the stronger “big image” hypothesis (
(

BI
0

)
) on
M
.
until the end of §
5
that
f
is residually non-Eisenstein. This, together with the fact that
g
is readily so, implies that we have a symmetric
\mathcal{O}
-linear perfect pairing

\langle\,,\,\rangle\,:\,M\times M\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(1)

such that
\langle x^{\sigma},y^{c\sigma c}\rangle=\langle x,y\rangle^{\sigma}
for all
x,y\in M
and
\sigma\in G_{K}
, where
c\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
denotes the choice of complex conjugation determined by our fixed embedding
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
. In particular, it follows that
M^{c}\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}}(M,\mathcal{O}(1))
and
A\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}}(M^{c},L/\mathcal{O}(1))
.

5.3.2.

Let
\mathcal{K}
be a finite extension of
K
,
v
a finite place of
\mathcal{K}
, and
\overline{v}=c(v)
. Then, we have an isomorphism
H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{\overline{v}},M)\cong H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v},M^{c})
. Therefore, cup-product gives a natural perfect pairing

H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{\overline{v}},M)\times H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v},A)\longrightarrow L/\mathcal{O}.

Similarly, we have a perfect pairing

H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{\overline{v}},M)\times H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v},M)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}.

Definition 5.10
.

Let
m
be a square-free product of primes coprime to
Np
and split in
K
. Let
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
.

The submodule
H^{1}_{\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)\subseteq H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
is defined as the kernel of the Coleman map

\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}M,\mathfrak{p},m}^{\bullet}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)\longrightarrow\Lambda[\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)].

For each positive integer
n
, the submodule
H^{1}_{\bullet}(K[mp^{n}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)\subseteq H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
is defined as the image of
H^{1}_{\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
under the natural projection

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)\longrightarrow H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M).

For each positive integer
n
, the local condition
H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[mp^{n}]_{\mathfrak{p}},M)\subseteq H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{\mathfrak{p}},M)
is defined as the kernel of the homomorphism

H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{\mathfrak{p}},M)\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)}{H^{1}_{\bullet}(K[mp^{n}]_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)}.

The submodule
H^{1}_{\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)\subseteq H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)
is defined as the kernel of the Coleman map

\mathrm{Col}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}M,\overline{\mathfrak{p}},m}^{\bullet}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)\longrightarrow\Lambda[\operatorname{Gal}(K[m]/K)].

For each positive integer
n
, the submodule
H^{1}_{\bullet}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)\subseteq H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)
is defined as the image of
H^{1}_{\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)
under the natural projection

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(K[mp^{\infty}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)\longrightarrow H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M).

For each positive integer
n
, the local condition
H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M)\subseteq H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M)
is defined by

H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M):=H^{1}_{\bullet}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)\subseteq H^{1}(K[mp^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M).

For
\mathfrak{q}\in\{\mathfrak{p},\overline{\mathfrak{p}}\}
and
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
,
we define
H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[p^{n}]_{\mathfrak{q}},A)\subseteq H^{1}(K[p^{n}]_{\mathfrak{q}},A)
as the orthogonal complement of
H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[p^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}},M)
under the pairing

H^{1}(K[p^{n}]_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}},M)\times H^{1}(K[p^{n}]_{\mathfrak{q}},A)\longrightarrow L/\mathcal{O}.

5.3.3.

Again, let
\mathcal{K}
be a finite extension of
K
and let
v
be a finite place of
\mathcal{K}
not dividing
p
. Then we define

H^{1}_{f}(\mathcal{K}_{v},V)=H^{1}_{\textup{ur}}(\mathcal{K}_{v},V)=\ker\left(H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v},V)\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v}^{\textup{ur}},V)\right).

We define
H^{1}_{f}(\mathcal{K}_{v},M)
and
H^{1}_{f}(\mathcal{K}_{v},A)
by propagation:

•

H^{1}_{f}(\mathcal{K}_{v},M)
is the preimage of
H^{1}_{f}(\mathcal{K}_{v},V)
under the map
H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v},M)\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v},V)
;

•

H^{1}_{f}(\mathcal{K}_{v},A)
is the image of
H^{1}_{f}(\mathcal{K}_{v},V)
under the map
H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v},V)\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathcal{K}_{v},A)
.

5.3.4.

For each positive integer
n
and for
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
, we define

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{n}],M)

\displaystyle=\ker\left(H^{1}(K[p^{n}],M)\longrightarrow\prod_{\lambda\nmid p}\frac{H^{1}(K[p^{n}]_{\lambda},M)}{H^{1}_{f}(K[p^{n}]_{\lambda},M)}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{q}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(K[p^{n}]_{\mathfrak{q}},M)}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[p^{n}]_{\mathfrak{q}},M)}\right),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sel}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{n}],A)

\displaystyle=\ker\left(H^{1}(K[p^{n}],A)\longrightarrow\prod_{\lambda\nmid p}\frac{H^{1}(K[p^{n}]_{\lambda},A)}{H^{1}_{f}(K[p^{n}]_{\lambda},A)}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{q}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(K[p^{n}]_{\mathfrak{q}},A)}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K[p^{n}]_{\mathfrak{q}},A)}\right).

We further define

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{n}],M),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sel}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}\mathrm{Sel}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{n}],A)

\displaystyle X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{Sel}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A),L/\mathcal{O})

Note that this definition of
H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
agrees with the definition given in Definition
5.4
.

Lemma 5.11
.

We have the following equalities:

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\sharp}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)=H^{1}_{\flat}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)=H^{1}_{f}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M),

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\sharp}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)=H^{1}_{\flat}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)=H^{1}_{f}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M).

Proof.

Let
z_{\infty}\in H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{q}},T\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))
, where
(\mathfrak{q},T)
is either
(\overline{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)
or
(\mathfrak{p},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
. There is an isomorphism

H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{q}},T\otimes\Lambda(\bm{\kappa}_{\textup{ac}}^{-1}))_{\Gamma}\cong H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{q}},T).

Let
z
be the image of
z_{\infty}
in
H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{q}},T)
under this isomorphism.
Let
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
. Then
z\in H^{1}_{\bullet}(K_{\mathfrak{q}},T)
if and only if
\mathrm{Col}^{\bullet}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1})=0
, where
\mathbb{1}
denotes the trivial character of
\Gamma
.
