By the interpolation formula of the Perrin-Riou map and (
5.1
) (or (
5.2
)), we have

\begin{bmatrix}\langle\mathcal{L}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1}),v_{\alpha}\rangle\\
\langle\mathcal{L}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1}),v_{\beta}\rangle\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\langle(1-\varphi)(1-p^{-1}\varphi^{-1})^{-1}\exp^{*}(z),v_{\alpha}\rangle\\
\langle(1-\varphi)(1-p^{-1}\varphi^{-1})^{-1}\exp^{*}(z),v_{\beta}\rangle\end{bmatrix}=(\alpha-\beta)Q_{g}^{-1}A_{g}\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{Col}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}^{\sharp}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1})\\
\mathrm{Col}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}^{\flat}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1})\end{bmatrix},

where we have written
v_{\xi}=v_{g,\xi}\otimes w_{\mathfrak{q},?}
for
\xi\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
,
?\in\{+,-\}
for simplicity.
Since the adjoint of
\varphi
is
p^{-1}\varphi^{-1}
, we deduce that

\chi_{g}(p)p\cdot\begin{bmatrix}\langle\exp^{*}(z),(1-\varphi)^{-1}(1-p^{-1}\varphi^{-1})(\varphi^{2}(v_{0})\otimes w_{\mathfrak{q}})\rangle\\
\langle\exp^{*}(z),-(1-\varphi)^{-1}(1-p^{-1}\varphi^{-1})(\varphi(v_{0})\otimes w_{\mathfrak{q}})\rangle\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{Col}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}^{\sharp}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1})\\
\mathrm{Col}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}^{\flat}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1})\end{bmatrix}.

On
\mathbb{D}_{\rm cris}(T)
, we have
\varphi^{2}+a\varphi+b=0
for some constants
a
and
b
. Then,
[
31
, proof of Lemma 5.6]
tells us that

(1-\varphi)^{-1}(1-p^{-1}\varphi^{-1})=\frac{(1+a+bp)\varphi+a(1+a+pb)+b(p-1)}{pb(1+a+b)}.

As
\langle u,v_{0}\otimes w_{\mathfrak{q}}\rangle=0
if
u
is in the image of
\exp^{*}
, we have

\begin{bmatrix}C_{\sharp}\langle\exp^{*}(z),\varphi(v_{0})\otimes w_{\mathfrak{q}}\rangle\\
C_{\flat}\langle\exp^{*}(z),\varphi(v_{0})\otimes w_{\mathfrak{q}}\rangle\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathrm{Col}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}^{\sharp}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1})\\
\mathrm{Col}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}^{\flat}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1})\end{bmatrix}

for some non-zero constants
C_{\sharp}
and
C_{\flat}
. Hence, we deduce that
\mathrm{Col}_{T,\mathfrak{q}}^{\bullet}(z_{\infty})(\mathbb{1})=0
if and only if
\exp^{*}(z)=0
, which is equivalent to
z\in H^{1}_{f}(K_{\mathfrak{q}},T)
.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
. The local conditions
H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M)
and
H^{1}_{\mathrm{ord},\bullet}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},M)
are exact orthogonal complements under the pairing

H^{1}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},M)\times H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},M)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
H^{1}_{\bullet}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)
and
H^{1}_{\bullet}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
are exact orthogonal complements under the pairing

H^{1}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)\times H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}.

But this follows from the previous lemma, since
H^{1}_{f}(K_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}M)
is the exact orthogonal complement of
H^{1}_{f}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}M)
under the above pairing by
[
6
, Proposition 3.8]
.
∎

We can now formulate signed main conjectures for the Selmer groups introduced above.

Conjecture 5.13
.

Let
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
. The class
z_{1}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
lies in
H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
. Moreover,
z_{1}^{\bullet}
is not
\Lambda
-torsion, both
H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
and
X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)
are rank-one
\Lambda
-modules and

\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)_{\mathrm{tors}}\right)=\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)}{\Lambda\cdot z_{1}^{\bullet}}\right)^{2}.

5.3.5.

Let
K(p^{\infty})
denote the ray class field of
K
of conductor
p^{\infty}
. In order to apply the Euler system machinery of
[
29
]
, we assume that the following set of “big image” hypotheses hold.

(
\mathbf{BI}
)

The following three conditions hold.

(
\mathbf{BI}_{0}
)

The residual
G_{K}
-representation
\overline{M}
is absolutely irreducible.

(
\mathbf{BI}_{1}
)

There exists
\sigma_{0}\in\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{K}/K(p^{\infty}))
such that
M/(\sigma_{0}-1)M\simeq\mathcal{O}
is a free
\mathcal{O}
-module of rank one.

(
\mathbf{BI}_{2}
)

There exists
\tau_{0}\in G_{K}
such that
\tau_{0}-1
acts on
M
as multiplication by a unit
a_{\tau_{0}}\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
with
a_{\tau_{0}}-1\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
denote the set of rational primes not dividing
pN
which split in
K
and let
\mathcal{S}
denote the set of square-free products of primes in
\mathcal{L}
. For each
q\in\mathcal{L}
, we fix a choice of prime
\mathfrak{q}
of
K
above
q
and we define

P_{\mathfrak{q}}(X)=\det(1-{\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}}X|V^{c}).

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
q\in\mathcal{L}
. Suppose that
m,mq\in\mathcal{S}
. Then, for
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
,

\mathrm{cor}_{K[mq]/K[m]}(z_{m}^{\bullet})=\chi_{f}(q)(\varphi_{0}(\overline{\mathfrak{q}}){\rm Fr}_{\overline{\mathfrak{q}}})^{2}P_{\mathfrak{q}}({\rm Fr}_{\mathfrak{q}})z_{m}^{\bullet}\quad\mathrm{mod}\,(q-1)H^{1}(K[mp^{\infty}],M).

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
4.11
.
∎

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
\bullet\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
. Assume that (
(LOC)
), (
(H

0

-

)
) and (
(

BI

)
) hold. Further, assume that
z_{1}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
is not
\Lambda
-torsion. Then both
H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)
and
X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)
are rank-one
\Lambda
-modules and

\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(X_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],A)_{\mathrm{tors}}\right)\supseteq\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[p^{\infty}],M)}{\Lambda\cdot z_{1}^{\bullet}}\right)^{2}.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.14
, the collection

\{z_{m}^{\bullet}\in H^{1}_{\bullet,\bullet}(K[mp^{\infty}],M)\,\colon\,m\in\mathcal{S}\}

is a split anticyclotomic Euler system for
M
in the sense of
[
29
]
.
This implies the desired inclusion by the Euler system machinery.
∎

Remark 5.16
.

Although the work
[
29
]
is not publicly available at the time of writing, the reader is invited to consult
[
2
, §8]
for an extensive review of the results therein.

Appendix A
A comparison of towers of Shimura curves

The tower of Shimura curves of level
U_{n}
naturally carries the Galois representations attached to self-dual twists of (ordinary families) modular forms. In this appendix, we compare its cohomology with the more classical (but for our purposes, rather ad hoc) definition using the tower of classical modular curves (that are associated to level subgroups
U_{1,n}
).

A.1.
A comparison at finite layers

Our comparison will go through the modular curve
X^{1,1}_{n}
(that covers both
X_{1,n}
and
X(U_{n})
) of level subgroup

U^{1,1}_{n}:=\{g\in U_{0}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\star\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\}\,.

We recall also the level subgroup

U_{0,n}:=\{g\in U_{0}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\star&\star\\
&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\}

and the corresponding Shimura curve
X_{0,n}
, as well the subgroup

U_{n}^{1}:=\{g\in U_{0}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\star\\
&\star\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n})\}\,.

We remark that both
X^{1,1}_{n}\xrightarrow{\,q_{1}\,}X_{1,n}
and
X^{1,1}_{n}\xrightarrow{\,q_{2}\,}X(U_{n})
are Galois coverings with cyclic Galois groups (recall that
p>2
) isomorphic to

(A.1)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} T_{n}^{(2)}:=U_{1,n}/U^{1,1}_{n}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\,\det\,}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\xleftarrow[\sim]{\,\det^{\frac{1}{2}}\,}U_{n}/U^{1,1}_{n}=Z_{n}\,.\end{aligned}

where the square-root of the determinant can be defined since
\det
maps
Z_{n}
isomorphically onto squares in the multiplicative group
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
. However, it depends on the choice of an element of order
2
in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
, equivalently a square-root of
g^{2}
where
g
is any topological generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. We declare
8
8

8

This choice will not affect the main constructions of this paper (that concern triple products).
henceforth that
(g^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}=g
(rather than
-g
). Let us also fix a generator
\boldsymbol{\zeta}:=\{\zeta_{p^{n}}\}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
.

A.1.1.

The cover
X^{1,1}_{n}\xrightarrow{\,q_{0}\,}X_{0,n}
is also Galois with Galois group

T_{n}:=U_{0,n}/U^{1,1}_{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}T_{n}^{(1)}\times T_{n}^{(2)}\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\det,\det)}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\,,

where
T_{n}^{(1)}:=U_{n}^{1}/U_{n}^{1,1}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\det}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
.

A.1.2.

Let us put
G_{n}:=\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{n}})/\mathbb{Q})
and denote by
\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle
the tautological Galois character

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle\,:\,G_{\mathbb{Q}}\twoheadrightarrow G_{n}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]\,.

We let
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]^{\iota}
denote the free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]
-module of rank one on which
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
acts via
\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-1}
. More explicitly,
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]^{\iota}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]
as a set, and
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q}}
acts multiplication by
g^{-1}
on the group like elements of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]
. Let us put

\chi_{n}\,:\,G_{n}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\,,\qquad g\mapsto\chi_{\textup{cyc}}(g)\mod p^{n}\,.

We denote by

\chi_{n}^{-1}:(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}G_{n}\,,\qquad g\mapsto\delta_{g}

its inverse (not the reciprocal of the character
\chi_{n}
). Via (
A.1
), §
A.1.1
and
\chi_{n}^{-1}
, we will identify the tautological character
T_{n}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[T_{n}^{(i)}]^{\times}
with
\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle
as well (and denote it by
\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{(i)}
when we wish to distinguish the choices
i=1,2
), so that we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{T_{n}^{(i)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{(i)}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\chi_{n}^{-1}\circ\,\det}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}[T_{n}^{(i)}]^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\chi_{n}^{-1}\circ\,\det}
\textstyle{G_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]^{\times}}

A.1.3.

Similarly, we denote by
Z_{n}\xrightarrow{\,\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{Z}\,}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[Z_{n}]^{\times}
the tautological character, and let
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[Z_{n}]^{\iota}
denote the free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[Z_{n}]
-module on which
Z_{n}
acts via
\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{Z}^{-1}
. Via the morphisms (
A.1
), we may endow
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[T_{n}^{(1)}]
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]
with the structure of a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[Z_{n}]
-module:

(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}Z_{n}\xrightarrow{\det}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times,2}\xrightarrow{\,\chi_{n}^{-1}\,}G_{n}^{2}\hookrightarrow G_{n}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]^{\times}\,,\qquad[g]=\begin{bmatrix}g&0\\
0&g\end{bmatrix}\mapsto\delta_{g^{2}}=\delta_{g}^{2}\,,

where for a multiplicative abelian group
G
, we write
G^{2}
to denote the squares in
G
. This tells us that we have

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{(i)}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]\simeq\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{2}_{Z}

as
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}]
-modules.

A.1.4.

We then have the Hecke equivariant natural isomorphisms

(A.2)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X^{1,1}_{n}\times\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{1,n}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[G_{n}]^{\iota}&\simeq H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{1,n}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{(1)}^{-1}\\
&\simeq H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{1,n}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{Z}^{-2}\,,\end{aligned}

of Galois modules, where the torus
T_{n}
acts diagonally on the tensor products on the first row (through
T_{n}\twoheadrightarrow T_{n}^{(2)}
on the first factor, and
T_{n}\twoheadrightarrow T_{n}^{(1)}
on the second). In (
A.2
), the first is induced from the isomorphism

X_{n}^{1,1}\simeq X_{1,n}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p^{n}})\simeq X_{1,n}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}[G_{n}]

which is determined by
\boldsymbol{\zeta}
and Shapiro’s lemma (the second can be described similarly).

A.1.5.

Let
\mathfrak{m}
be a maximal ideal of the Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}_{n}^{B}
acting on
H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, and let us denote its pullback to the Hecke algebra acting on
H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{n}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
also by
\mathfrak{m}
. Let us assume that
\mathfrak{m}
is non-Eisenstein in the sense that
\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime}-(q+1)\not\in\mathfrak{m}
for some prime
q\nmid Np^{n}
. The following lemma is borrowed from
[
21
]
.

Lemma A.1
.

For any non-Eisenstein maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
as above, we have the following Hecke equivariant isomorphism of Galois modules:

(A.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{n}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}[T_{n}]}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[T_{n}/Z_{n}]\,.\end{aligned}

Proof.

This is proved as part of the proof of
[
21
, Corollary 3.2]
, where the key point is that, for any intermediate curve
X_{n}^{1,1}\to Y\to X(U_{n})
, the Hecke module
H^{2}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(Y\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{m}}
is annihilated by the degree-0 Hecke operator
\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime}-(q+1)
, which also acts invertibly on the said module since
\mathrm{T}_{q}^{\prime}-(q+1)\not\in\mathfrak{m}
.
∎

Remark A.2
.

In
[
21
]
, the author does not require the non-Eisenstein condition, but instead applies the ordinary projector. Our lemma slightly extends Fouquet’s corollary as we do not pass to the
p
-ordinary direct summand of the cohomology, at the expense of excluding the Eisenstein locus.

Let us assume for all the Shimura curves in this paragraph that the level away from
p
is
U_{0}(N)

(
rather than
U_{1}(N)
)
. We have a decomposition
9
9

9

In general, the constituents in the analogous decomposition of
H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{n}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}[T_{n}]}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[T_{n}/Z_{n}]
will involve twists of Deligne’s representations that are self-dual up to a Dirichlet character of conductor dividing
N
.

H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{n}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}[T_{n}]}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[T_{n}/Z_{n}]=:H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{n}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\dagger}\simeq\bigoplus_{g}V_{g}^{\dagger}

into Hecke eigenspaces, where the direct sum is over all weight-two eigenforms
g
on
U_{1,n}
and
V_{g}^{\dagger}
, as in
[
27
]
, is the self-dual twist of Deligne’s representation. Therefore, the Galois module
H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{n}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\dagger}
coincides with the Hecke module
H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{1,n}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})\left(-\mathinner{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\frac{1}{\phantom{2}}$}\cr\raisebox{-0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\frac{\phantom{1}}{2}$}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\frac{1}{\phantom{2}}$}\cr\raisebox{-0.75pt}{$\textstyle\frac{\phantom{1}}{2}$}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\frac{1}{\phantom{2}}$}\cr\raisebox{-0.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\frac{\phantom{1}}{2}$}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\frac{1}{\phantom{2}}$}\cr\raisebox{-0.75pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\frac{\phantom{1}}{2}$}\cr}}}}\right)
in the notation of
[
18
]
. The promised relationship between the cohomology of towers
X(U_{n})
and
X_{1,n}
can be extracted from (
A.2
) and (
A.3
).

A.2.
Hida theory: A variant of Ohta’s control theorem and a result of Fouquet

Recall that
p
is a prime which does not divide
N^{-}
and that we have identified
\Psi_{p}:\mathcal{O}_{B}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\simeq M_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

A.2.1.

Let
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be an unramified finite extension, with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}
. Let
T_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}
, resp.
Z_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}
, denote the maximal diagonal torus, resp. center, of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
. Then we identify
T_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/Z_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
via the map

T_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/Z_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\,\qquad\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\\
&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mapsto ad^{-1},

and let
\Lambda_{Z}:=\mathcal{O}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/Z_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket\simeq\mathcal{O}\llbracket\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rrbracket
be the corresponding Iwasawa algebra; its
n
-th layer will be denoted by
\Lambda_{Z,n}
. Note that

\Lambda_{Z,n}\simeq\mathcal{O}[T_{n}/Z_{n}],

where
T_{n}
and
Z_{n}
have been introduced in §
A.1
. Note in addition that, if
\Lambda_{\rm full}:=\mathcal{O}\llbracket T_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rrbracket
, the natural projection
T_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\twoheadrightarrow T_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/Z_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
induces a morphism
\Lambda_{\rm full}\to\Lambda_{Z}
.

A.2.2.

We define

H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O}):=\varprojlim_{n}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O}),

where the inverse limit is taken with respect to the pushforward of the natural degeneracy maps
X(U_{n+1})\xrightarrow{p_{n}}X(U_{n})
. We note that
H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})
is naturally a
\Lambda_{Z}
-module. Moreover, as the Hecke operator
{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}
commutes with the pushforward maps
p_{n,*}
, it acts on the inverse limit. Hence, the anti-ordinary projector
e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}=\lim({\rm U}_{p}^{\prime})^{n!}
acts on
H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})
.

A.2.3.

As a special case of the work
[
33
]
, we shall prove (as Proposition
A.4
(iii) below) a control theorem for
e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})
. Moreover, we shall record (cf. Proposition
A.4
(i) below) the fact (borrowed from
[
21
]
, Corollary 3.2) that the module
e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})
is the essentially self-dual twist (in the sense of
[
27
, Definition 2.1.3]
; where “essentially self-dual” means self-dual up to Dirichlet characters of conductor coprime to
p
) of the big Galois representations associated to Hida families.

A.2.4.

Before we state these, we introduce some notation. For a weight
\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2})
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
, we let
V_{\lambda}
denote the irreducible representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
(over
K
) of highest weight
\lambda
. If
V_{\rm std}\simeq K^{2}
denotes the standard representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
over
K
, then

V_{\lambda}={\rm Sym}^{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}(V)\otimes{\rm det}^{\lambda_{2}}.

Let
V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}}
be the minimal admissible lattice of
V_{\lambda}
, which is explicitly given by

V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}}={\rm TSym}^{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}(\mathcal{O}^{2})\otimes{\rm det}^{\lambda_{2}},

and denote by
\mathscr{V}_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}}
the corresponding local system on
X(U_{n})
.

Remark A.3
.

Note that if
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
is trivial on
Z_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}
, then
\lambda_{2}=-\lambda_{1}
and
V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}}={\rm TSym}^{2\lambda_{1}}(\mathcal{O}^{2})\otimes{\rm det}^{-\lambda_{1}}.

Let us denote by

\mathrm{T}^{\rm a.o.}_{B,\mathcal{O}}(Np^{\infty}):=\varprojlim_{n}e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}\mathbb{T}_{n}^{B}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}

the anti-ordinary Hecke algebra.

Proposition A.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}
be a maximal ideal of
\mathrm{T}^{\rm a.o.}_{B,\mathcal{O}}(Np^{\infty})
such that the associated mod-
p
Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is
irreducible and has residual multiplicity
1
(in the sense of
[
21
]
, Proposition 3.7(iii)).

We have an isomorphism of
\Lambda_{Z}
-modules

e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X_{\infty}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{\rm full}}\Lambda_{Z}\,,

where

H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X_{\infty}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O}):=\varprojlim_{n}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X_{n}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})

and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
denotes the pullback of
\mathfrak{m}
to the Hecke algebra
\mathrm{T}^{\rm a.o.}_{B,\mathcal{O}}(U_{\infty}^{1,1})
acting on
e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X_{\infty}^{1,1}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})
.

The
\Lambda_{Z}
-module
e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}
is finitely generated and free. Moreover,

e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\mathrm{T}^{\rm a.o.}_{B,\mathcal{O}}(Np^{\infty})_{\mathfrak{m}}}R(\mathfrak{a})

is of rank 2 over
R(\mathfrak{a}):=\mathrm{T}^{\rm a.o.}_{B,\mathcal{O}}(Np^{\infty})_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{a}
, where
\mathfrak{a}
is the unique minimal prime ideal contained in
\mathfrak{m}
.

Let
\lambda
be a weight which is trivial on
Z_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}
and let
\mathcal{O}[\lambda^{-1}]
be
\mathcal{O}
regarded with
\Lambda_{Z}
-action given by
\lambda^{-1}
. For every
n\geq 1
, we have an isomorphism of
\Lambda_{Z,n}
-modules

e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{Z,n}}\mathcal{O}[\lambda^{-1}]\simeq e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathscr{V}_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}},

which is compatible with Galois and Hecke actions.

Proof.

The first part is a special case of
[
21
, Corollary 3.2]
, whereas the second follows from (i) combined with
[
21
, Proposition 3.7]
. We now prove (iii).
By
[
33
, Theorem 2.6.2]
, together with the fact that localization is exact and commutes with the Tor-functor, we have a convergent spectral sequence

E^{i,j}_{2,\mathfrak{m}}={\rm Tor}^{\Lambda_{Z,n}}_{-i}(e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{j}_{\rm Iw}(X(U_{\infty})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathcal{O})_{\mathfrak{m}},\mathcal{O}[\lambda^{-1}])\Rightarrow e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{i+j}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathscr{V}_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}}\,,

which is supported on the second quadrant
i\leq 0
,
j\geq 0
. Since
E^{i,j}_{2,\mathfrak{m}}=0
unless
j=1
, we have the desired isomorphism
E^{0,1}_{2,\mathfrak{m}}\simeq e_{{\rm U}_{p}^{\prime}}H^{1}_{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X(U_{n})\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\mathscr{V}_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})_{\mathfrak{m}}
. The proof of our proposition is now complete.
∎

Appendix B
The definite case: signed balanced
p
-adic
L
-functions

The main purpose of this appendix is to present an alternative construction of Hsieh’s diagonal theta elements for Hida families using a representation-theoretic approach, similar to the construction of diagonal cycles presented in §
2
(which is essentially due to Loeffler). Instead of working with indefinite quaternion algebras, we work with definite ones.

Even though this portion of the appendix has no utility in the main body of the article, it is included here to highlight the parallelism between Hsieh’s construction of theta elements in terms of the special locus on (zero-dimensional) Shimura sets in the definite scenario and the construction of diagonal cycles in the indefinite setting. This resemblance suggests the possibility of the existence of a bipartite Euler system (in the sense of Bertolini–Darmon and Howard
[
4
,
26
]
), but we do not explore this point further in the present article. Furthermore, some of the ideas presented here seem pertinent to the upcoming work of Yi-Li Wu on signed triple-product
p
-adic
L
-functions, which, combined with our construction of signed diagonal classes, may yield a signed version of the explicit reciprocity law.

B.1.
A family of definite theta elements

Let
B
be the definite quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
of discriminant
N^{-}
. Let
N^{+}
be a positive integer prime to
N^{-}
and let
N=N^{+}N^{-}
. We suppose that
p
is an odd prime such that
p\nmid N
. In what follows, we retain the notation in §
2.1
. In particular, recall that
E=\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mathbb{Q}
denotes the totally split étale cubic algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
, set
B_{E}=B\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}E=B\oplus B\oplus B
, and denote by
\iota\,:\,B\hookrightarrow B_{E}
the diagonal embedding. We also recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{Z,(n)}

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in\mathcal{U}_{1}(N)\,:\,g\equiv\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{1}&b\\
0&a_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{2}&c\\
0&a_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{3}&d\\
0&a_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n}),\,a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\,b,c,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right\}

for any natural number
n
(as well as our convention that
(n)
is a shorthand for the triple
(n,n,n)
).

Definition B.1
.

For every
n\geq 0
, let

S_{0}(Np^{n})
be the Shimura set

S_{0}(Np^{n}):=B^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}^{\times}/U_{0,n},

with
U_{0,n}
as in (
2.4
);

\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}
be the “non-split triple product” of Shimura sets given by

\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}=B_{E}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}/\mathcal{U}_{Z,(n)}.

B.1.1.

For each element
(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\in\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}
, we write
[(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})]
for the corresponding double coset in
\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}
. As in
[
28
, Definition 4.6]
, we define
\Delta_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}]
by setting

(B.1)

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}:=\sum_{[g]\in S_{0}(Np^{n})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\\
z\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\end{subarray}}\left[\left(g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{n}&b\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),g\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{n}&b+z\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),g\tau_{p^{n}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&z\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\right]\,,

where
\tau_{p^{n}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
-p^{n}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

Remark B.2
.

Note that the Shimura set
\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}
differs from the one used in
[
28
, §4.6]
, which is given as

B_{E}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}/\mathcal{U}_{1,(n)}\iota(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\times})\,.

Although this definition of
\Delta_{n}
allows more freedom in principle, this distinction does not affect applications, as one eventually evaluates the theta elements at triples of modular forms whose central characters multiply to the trivial character.

B.1.2.

Let
\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}
be the Hecke operator defined as in §
2.2.3
. Its action on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}]
is associated with the pushforward of
\iota(\eta_{p})\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{3}
, where we recall that
\eta_{p}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
(this operator is denoted as
\mathbf{U}_{p}
in
[
28
, §4.4]
).

Proposition B.3

(
[
28
]
, Lemma 4.7)
.

Let us denote by
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n+1)}]\xrightarrow{{\rm Nm}_{n}^{n+1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}]
the natural projection. Then for every
n\geq 1
, we have

{\rm Nm}_{n}^{n+1}(\Delta_{n+1})=\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,\Delta_{n}.

As before, let us denote by
e_{\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}}
the anti-ordinary projector associated with
\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}
, and define the regularized definite theta element on setting

\Delta^{\dagger}_{n}:=(\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n}(e_{\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}}\,\Delta_{n}),

as well as the element

\Delta^{\dagger}_{\infty}:=\varprojlim_{n\to\infty}\Delta^{\dagger}_{n}\in\varprojlim_{n\to\infty}e_{\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}],

where the inverse limit is taken with respect to the maps
{\rm Nm}_{n}
given as above.

B.2.
A variant of Hsieh’s construction à la Loeffler

In §
2
, we constructed families of diagonal cycles arising from the embedding of a Shimura curve into its triple product by essentially mimicking the definition of
\Delta^{\dagger}_{\infty}
. However, our construction allows more freedom in terms of
p
-adic variation. This was achieved by exploiting the general machinery of Loeffler developed in
[
36
]
.

With this in mind, our goal in the present subsection is to give an alternative definition of the element
\Delta_{n}
defined as in (
B.1
), which in turn will allow a direct comparison with the cycles of Definition
2.6
. The proof of Proposition
B.3
will then be an application of the main result of
[
36
]
.

B.2.1.

We introduce basic notation that we shall dwell on in this subsection, and prove a simple lemma.

Let
S_{n}
be the Shimura set of level
U_{Z,n}
, defined as in (
2.3
). As
U_{Z,n}\subseteq U_{0,n}
, we have a natural map
S_{n}\xrightarrow{\pi_{Z,n}}S_{0}(Np^{n})
of Shimura sets. Let us denote by
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}]\xrightarrow{\pi_{Z,n}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{0}(Np^{n})]
also the induced morphism and by
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{0}(Np^{n})]\xrightarrow{\nu_{Z,n}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}]
the trace map induced by

S_{0}(Np^{n})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}],\,\qquad[x]\mapsto\sum_{\pi_{Z,n}([y])=[x]}[y]\,.

For every
(z,b)\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
, let us choose a lift to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, which we still denote by
(z,b)
, and define
\gamma_{z,b}:=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}z&b\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Lemma B.4
.

A set of left coset representatives for the quotient
U_{0,n}/U_{Z,n}
is given by

\{\gamma_{z,b}:\,(z,b)\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\}.

Proof.

This follows from a direct computation. Note first that two different elements
\gamma_{z,b}
and
\gamma_{z^{\prime},b^{\prime}}
define different left cosets. We now check that any given
g\in U_{0,n}
can be written as
\gamma_{z,b}\cdot h
, for some
(z,b)\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
and
h\in U_{Z,n}
, or equivalently that there exists
(z,b)\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
such that
\gamma_{z,b}^{-1}\cdot g\in U_{Z,n}
. Indeed, if
g\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
&\delta\end{smallmatrix}\right)\pmod{p^{n}}
, we may put
(z,b)=(\alpha\delta^{-1},\beta\delta^{-1})
.
∎

By Lemma
B.4
, the trace map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{0}(Np^{n})]\xrightarrow{\nu_{Z,n}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}]
is explicitly given by
[x]\mapsto\sum_{(z,b)}[x\cdot\gamma_{z,b}]
.

B.2.2.

For any triple
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
, we also let
\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}
and
\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}^{\mathbf{n}}}
be the “non-split triple product” Shimura sets associated with the level subgroups
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\mathbf{n}}
introduced in §
2.1.2
. We recall that

u=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{3}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

This element, together with the diagonal embedding
\iota
, gives rise to a map of Shimura sets

\iota^{u}_{(n),\star}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}]\xrightarrow{u_{\star}\circ\iota_{\star}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}],

where we recall that
(n)=(n,n,n)
. Composing
\iota^{u}_{(n),\star}
with the map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}]\xrightarrow{\iota(\eta^{n}_{p})_{\star}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}]
induced by right-multiplication by
\iota(\eta^{n}_{p})
, we obtain a morphism

\iota(\eta^{n}_{p})_{\star}\circ\iota^{u}_{(n),\star}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}].

Paralleling our Definition
2.6
, we introduce the following element.

Definition B.5
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we define
\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}:=\iota(\eta^{n}_{p})_{\star}\circ\iota^{u}_{(n),\star}(\mathbb{1}_{S_{n}})\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}]
, where
\mathbb{1}_{S_{n}}=\sum_{g\in S_{n}}[g]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}]
.

For every
{\mathbf{n}}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
, we define
\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}:={\rm Nm}^{(n)}_{\mathbf{n}}\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}
, where
n=\max\{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\}
and
{\rm Nm}^{(n)}_{\mathbf{n}}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}]\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}]
denotes the natural projection.

For every
{\mathbf{n}}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
, let us consider the natural morphism (cf. §
2.2.4
)

\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\xrightarrow{\,{\rm b}_{\mathbf{n}}\,}\mathbf{S}_{\mathbf{n}}=\mathbf{S}_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}}:=S_{U_{n_{1}}}\times S_{U_{n_{2}}}\times S_{U_{n_{3}}}\,,

and define

(B.2)

\Theta_{\mathbf{n}}:={\rm b}_{\mathbf{n},*}\,\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n_{1}}}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n_{2}}}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n_{3}}}]\,.

Thanks to
[
36
, Prop. 4.5.2]
(see also Proposition
2.8
), the following norm-compatibility property holds.

Proposition B.6
.

For every triple
{\mathbf{n}}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
of positive integers we have

{\rm Nm}^{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{n}}(\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n+1}}})=\mathbb{U}_{p}^{\prime}\,\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{n}}}\,.

B.2.3.

We explain how to compare explicitly the element
\Delta_{n}
given as in (
B.1
) and the element
\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}
of Definition
B.5
.

Mirroring our discussion in §
B.2.2
, the diagonal embedding
\iota
, together with right multiplication by
u
, we define a map

\iota_{Z,(n),\star}^{u}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}]\xrightarrow{u_{\star}\circ\iota_{\star}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}^{Z}_{(n)}}]

of Shimura sets, where
\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}^{Z}_{(n)}}=B_{E}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}/{\mathcal{U}^{Z}_{(n)}}
and

\mathcal{U}^{Z}_{(n)}:=\left\{g\in\mathcal{U}_{1}(N)\,:\,g\equiv\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{1}&0\\
b&a_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{2}&0\\
c&a_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a_{3}&0\\
d&a_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\,(\text{mod }p^{n}),\,a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\,b,c,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right\}\,,

i.e.,
{\mathcal{U}^{Z}_{(n)}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{1}(N)
is the subgroup with conditions modulo
p^{n}
transposed to those defining
{\mathcal{U}_{Z,(n)}}
. We therefore have a map

\iota(\eta^{n}_{p})_{\star}\circ\iota_{Z,(n),\star}^{u}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}].

Proposition B.7
.

For every natural number
n
, we have

\Delta_{n}=\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\star}\circ\iota_{Z,(n),\star}^{u}\left(\mathbb{1}_{S_{n}}\right).

Proof.

Recall that
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{0}(Np^{n})]\xrightarrow{\nu_{Z,n}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}]
denotes the trace map induced by

S_{0}(Np^{n})\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{n}],\,\qquad[x]\mapsto\sum_{\pi_{Z,n}([y])=[x]}[y]\,.

Crucially, thanks to Lemma
B.4
,
\nu_{Z,n}
is explicitly given by
[x]\mapsto\sum_{(z,b)}[x\cdot\gamma_{z,b}]
.

Observe that

\iota(\gamma_{z,b})\cdot u\cdot\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}zp^{n}&b\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}zp^{n}&z+b\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-bp^{n}&z\\
-p^{n}&\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\,,

and that the third component can be written as
\tau_{p^{n}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
-bp^{n}&z\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Then, we have the right-
\mathcal{U}_{Z,(n)}
congruence

\iota(\gamma_{z,b})\cdot u\cdot\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})\,\mathcal{U}_{Z,(n)}=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{n}&b\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{n}&z+b\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\tau_{p^{n}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&z\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\,\mathcal{U}_{Z,(n)}\,.

Therefore, by Lemma
B.4
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{[g]\in S_{0}(Np^{n})}\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\star}\circ\iota_{Z,(n),\star}^{u}\circ\nu_{Z,n}[g]

\displaystyle=\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\star}\circ\iota_{Z,(n),\star}^{u}\left(\sum_{[g]\in S_{0}(Np^{n})}\nu_{Z,n}[g]\right)

\displaystyle=\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\star}\circ\iota_{Z,(n),\star}^{u}\left(\sum_{[g]\in S_{n}}[g]\right)

\displaystyle=\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})_{\star}\circ\iota_{Z,(n),\star}^{u}\left(\mathbb{1}_{S_{n}}\right),

as claimed.
∎

Corollary B.8
.

For every natural number
n
, we have

{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{Z}\Theta_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}=\Delta_{n}\,,

where
{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{Z}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}]\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathbf{S}_{{Z,(n)}}]
denotes the natural projection.

Proof.

Since

u\,\mathcal{U}^{(n)}u^{-1}\cap\iota(\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times})=u\,\mathcal{U}^{Z}_{(n)}u^{-1}\cap\iota(\mathcal{O}_{B,f}^{\times})=U_{Z,{n}},

we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{S_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{(n)}^{u}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}_{(n)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}^{(n)}_{Z}}
\textstyle{S_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{Z,(n)}^{u}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{S}_{\mathcal{U}^{Z}_{(n)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota(\eta_{p}^{n})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{S}_{Z,(n)}.}

The result then follows from this, together with Proposition
B.7
.
∎

B.3.
Balanced triple product
p
-adic
L
-functions

We recall the definition of balanced
p
-adic triple-product
L
-functions from
[
28
, §4.7]
, which are defined in terms of the theta element
\Delta^{\dagger}_{\infty}
.

We let

(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\in e\mathbf{S}(N_{1},\psi_{1},\mathbf{I}_{1})\times e\mathbf{S}(N_{2},\psi_{2},\mathbf{I}_{2})\times e\mathbf{S}(N_{3},\psi_{3},\mathbf{I}_{3})

denote a triple of primitive Hida families of tame conductors
(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3})
and branch characters
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\psi_{3})
, where
e=e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}}
and each
\mathbf{I}_{i}
is a normal domain finite flat over the Iwasawa algebra
\mathcal{O}\llbracket 1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rrbracket
(here
\mathcal{O}
is the ring of integers of a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
). Let us put
\mathcal{R}=\mathbf{I}_{1}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{I}_{2}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{I}_{3}
and assume that

•

\psi_{1}\psi_{2}\psi_{3}=\omega^{2a}
, where
a\in\mathbb{Z}
,

•

{\rm gcd}(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3})
is square-free.

Let us put
N={\rm lcm}(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3})
. We denote by
\Sigma^{-}
the set of primes
\ell\mid N
where the local root number of the triple-product
L
-function attached to the specialisation of
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})
at some (therefore, by automorphic rigidity, any) arithmetic point equals
-1
. We further assume that

\mathbf{JL}
)

\bullet

\#\Sigma^{-}
is odd,

\bullet

N^{-}:=\prod_{\ell\in\Sigma^{-}}\ell
and
N/N^{-}
are relatively prime,

\bullet

the residual Galois representation attached to each of the Hida families
\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g},
and
\mathbf{h}
is absolutely irreducible,
p
-distinguished, and it is ramified at every
\ell\in\Sigma^{-}
with
\ell\equiv 1
(
\text{mod }p
).

Under these hypotheses, we consider the definite quaternion algebra
B
over
\mathbb{Q}
with discriminant
N^{-}
and consider the primitive Jacquet-Langlands lift to
B_{E}
of
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})
(cf.
[
28
]
, Theorem 4.5):

(\mathbf{f}^{B},\mathbf{g}^{B},\mathbf{h}^{B})\in e\mathbf{S}^{B}(N_{1},\psi_{1},\mathbf{I}_{1})\times e\mathbf{S}^{B}(N_{2},\psi_{2},\mathbf{I}_{2})\times e\mathbf{S}^{B}(N_{3},\psi_{3},\mathbf{I}_{3}).

Definition B.9
.

We define
\mathbf{F}^{B}=\mathbf{f}^{B}\boxtimes\mathbf{g}^{B}\boxtimes\mathbf{h}^{B}:B_{E}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}
as the triple product given by

\mathbf{F}^{B}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=\mathbf{f}^{B}(x_{1})\otimes\mathbf{g}^{B}(x_{2})\otimes\mathbf{h}^{B}(x_{3}).

Let
\chi_{\mathcal{R}}^{*}:\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}^{\times}
be the character given by

z\mapsto\omega^{a}(z)\langle\epsilon_{\textup{cyc}}(z)\rangle^{-3}\langle\epsilon_{\textup{cyc}}(z)\rangle_{\mathbf{I}_{1}}^{1/2}\langle\epsilon_{\textup{cyc}}(z)\rangle_{\mathbf{I}_{2}}^{1/2}\langle\epsilon_{\textup{cyc}}(z)\rangle_{\mathbf{I}_{3}}^{1/2}.

We define
\mathbf{F}^{B,\dagger}:B_{E}^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}_{E}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}
by the formula

\mathbf{F}^{B,\dagger}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=\mathbf{F}^{B}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\chi_{\mathcal{R}}^{*}(\nu(x_{3})),

where
\nu
is the reduced norm on
B
.

The theta element attached to
\mathbf{F}^{B,\dagger}
is defined by

\Theta_{\mathbf{F}^{B,\dagger}}:=\mathbf{F}^{B,\dagger}(\Delta_{\infty}^{\dagger})\in\mathcal{R}.

The theta element
\Theta_{\mathbf{F}^{B,\dagger}}
is Hsieh’s balanced
p
-adic
L
-function. Its interpolative properties, which justify this nomenclature, are recorded in
[
28
, Corollary 4.13]
. It is worth recalling that the interpolation formula proven by Hsieh relates the square of the specialization of
\Theta_{\mathbf{F}^{B,\dagger}}
at each arithmetic point
(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
in the balanced range with the algebraic part of the critical central
L
-value attached to
(\mathbf{f}_{P_{1}},\mathbf{g}_{P_{2}},\mathbf{h}_{P_{3}})
.

B.4.
Signed balanced
p
-adic
L
-function

We retain the notation of §
B.1
–
B.3
. Let
f\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{1}(N_{1})\cap U_{1},\psi_{1})
be a
p
-old ordinary eigenform. We also let
g\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{1}(N_{2}),\psi_{2})
be a new form with
\mathrm{ord}_{p}\,a_{p}(g)>0
. Finally, we let
\textup{\bf h}\in eS(N_{3},\psi_{3},\mathbf{I})
be a Hida family as in the previous section. We assume that
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\psi_{3})
and
(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3})
satisfy the conditions of §
B.3
. For the definite quaternion algebra
B
as in loc. cit., we then have a primitive Jacquet–Langlands lift

(f^{B},g^{B},\textup{\bf h}^{B})\in eS^{B}(N_{1}p,\psi_{1})\times S^{B}(N_{2},\psi_{2})\times\mathbf{S}^{B}(N_{3},\psi_{3},\mathbf{I})

of the triple
(f,g,\textup{\bf h})
.

B.4.1.

For a triple
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})
of natural numbers, we define the element

\Theta_{{\mathbf{n}}}^{\ddagger}:=((\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n_{1}}e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}},{\rm id},(\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime})^{-n_{3}}e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}})\,\Theta_{{\mathbf{n}}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n_{1}}}]^{\rm ord}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n_{2}}}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n_{3}}}]^{\rm ord}\,,

where
\Theta_{\bf n}
is as in (
B.2
), and for any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra
A
and positive integer
m
we have set
A[S_{U_{m}}]^{\rm ord}:=e_{\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}}\cdot A[S_{U_{m}}]
. We let

\Theta_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}(g)\in\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n_{1}}}]^{\rm ord}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n_{3}}}]^{\rm ord}

denote the image of
\Theta_{n_{1},0,n_{3}}^{\ddagger}

under the map induced from
S_{U_{0}}:=S_{U_{1}(N)}\xrightarrow{\,g^{B}\,}\mathcal{O}
. We then define
\Theta_{n}^{\ddagger}(g)\in\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{1}}]^{\rm ord}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\rm ord}
as the image of
\Theta_{n,0,n}(g)
under the natural morphism
({\rm pr}^{n,0,n}_{1,0,n})
.

Finally, we let
\Theta_{n}(f,g)\in\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\rm ord}
denote the image of
\Theta_{n}^{\ddagger}(g)
under the map induced from
S_{U_{1}}\xrightarrow{\,f^{B}\,}\mathcal{O}
.

B.4.2.

Let us consider the subgroup
U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}\supseteq U_{n}
and let
S_{U_{n}}\xrightarrow{\varpi_{n}}S_{U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}}
be the corresponding map of Shimura sets. We denote by

\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}}]\xrightarrow{\nu_{n}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n}}],\,\,\,\qquad[x]\mapsto\sum_{[y]\,:\,\varpi_{n}([y])=[x]}[y]

the corresponding trace map. Note that

U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}/U_{n}\simeq(1+p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\simeq\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}.

For each
a\in(1+p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, let us choose a lift (which we again denote by
a
) to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. Then, a system of left coset representatives for
\left(U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}\right)/U_{n}
is given by

\left\{\gamma_{a}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,:\,a\in(1+p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\right\}.

Remark B.10
.

The trace map

\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}}]\xrightarrow{\nu_{n}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n}}]

can be written as

[x]\mapsto\sum_{a\in(1+p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})}[x\cdot\gamma_{a}]=\sum_{a\in(1+p^{n-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})/(1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\langle a\rangle\cdot[x],

where
\langle a\rangle
is the diamond operator given as in §
2.2.1
.

Lemma B.11
.

Let
n\geq 2
be an integer. Then the natural map
\pi_{n,n-1}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}}]\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}}]
induces an isomorphism

\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}\xrightarrow{\,\,\sim\,\,}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}.

Proof.

The proof is a standard argument in Hida theory, see for instance
[
20
]
. We give the details for the convenience of the reader.
The map
\pi_{n,n-1}
is surjective and its kernel is generated as a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module by the elements of the form

[xg]-[x]\quad\text{with }[x]\in S_{U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}}\text{ and }g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\text{ such that }p^{n-1}\mid\gcd(c,a-d)\,.

It therefore suffices to show that the operator
\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}
annihilates these elements.

To that end, let
[x]
and
g
be as above. Then

(B.3)

\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}([xg]-[x])=\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}\left(\left[xg\begin{pmatrix}p&j\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\right]-\left[x\begin{pmatrix}p&j\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\right]\right),

where the matrices
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p&j\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
are regarded as elements in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}
be the unique element with
k\equiv a^{-1}b\pmod{p}
. Then an easy computation shows that

\begin{pmatrix}p&j\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}p&j+k\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\in U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}\qquad\forall\,j\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}.

It follows that

\left[xg\begin{pmatrix}p&j+k\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\right]-\left[x\begin{pmatrix}p&j\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\right]=0\qquad\forall\,j\in\{0,1,\ldots,p-1\}

as elements in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}}]
. Combined with equation (
B.3
), this yields

\mathrm{U}_{p}^{\prime}([xg]-[x])=0,

as desired.
∎

For each integer
n\geq 2
, we denote by

\textup{res}_{n-1}^{n}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}

the composition of the inverse of the isomorphism
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}
in Lemma
B.11
with the trace map
\nu_{n}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n-1}\cap U_{0,n}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}
.

The following
Q
-system relation parallels that of the diagonal cycles (cf. Propositions
2.17
and
3.8
).

Proposition B.12
.

For any integer
n\geq 2
, we have

{\rm pr}_{n}^{n+1}\,\Theta_{n+1}(f,g)=a_{p}(g)\cdot\Theta_{n}(f,g)-\chi_{g}(p)\cdot\textup{res}_{n-1}^{n}\Theta_{n-1}(f,g)\,.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is similar to that of Proposition
3.12
, and we indicate the key steps here for the convenience of the reader. As in Proposition
3.8
, we have

(B.4)

{\rm pr}_{n}^{n+1}\,\Theta_{n+1}(f,g)=a_{p}(g)\cdot\Theta_{n}(f,g)-\chi_{g}(p)\cdot\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(f,g)\,,

(B.5)

{\rm pr}_{n}^{n+1}\,\widetilde{\Theta}_{n+1}(f,g)=p\cdot\Theta_{n}(f,g)\,.

Here,
\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(f,g)\in\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\rm ord}
is defined (paralleling our discussion in §
2.4.4
) as the image of
\Theta_{(n,1,n)}^{\ddagger}
under the compositum of the following maps:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\rm ord}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{1}}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\rm ord}\xrightarrow{({\rm pr}_{1}^{n},\pi_{2,*},{\rm id})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{1}}]^{\rm ord}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{0}}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\rm ord}\xrightarrow{(f,g,{\rm id})}\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\rm ord}\,.

The argument of Proposition
2.17
applies formally to show that

(B.6)

\langle 1,1,d\rangle\,\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(f,g)=\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(f,g)

for any integer
d\equiv 1\pmod{p^{n-1}}
. Note that, thanks to Remark
B.10
, (
B.6
) implies that
\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(f,g)
belongs to the image of the trace map
\nu_{n}
. Then, arguing as in the proof of Proposition
3.11
(and relying on Lemma
B.11
), we deduce using (
B.5
) that

(B.7)

\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(f,g)=\textup{res}_{n-1}^{n}\Theta_{n-1}(f,g)\,.

The proof of our proposition now follows by combining (
B.4
) and (
B.7
).
∎

B.4.3.

As in the main body of our article, let us denote by
\alpha
and
\beta
the roots of the Hecke polynomial
X^{2}-a_{p}(g)X+\chi_{g}(p)p
of
g
at
p
. As in Definition
4.5
, we define the
p
-stabilized class

\Theta_{n,\xi}(f,g)=\frac{1}{\xi^{n}}\left(\Theta_{n}(f,g)-\frac{\chi_{g}(p)}{\xi}\textup{res}_{n-1}^{n}\Theta_{n-1}(f,g)\right)\,\in\,L[S_{U_{n}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}\,,\qquad\xi=\alpha,\beta\,,\quad n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}\,.

Denote

U_{\infty}:=\{g\in U_{1}(N)\,:\,\Psi_{p}(g_{p})=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
&a\end{smallmatrix}\right),\,\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},b\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\}.

We then have the following “definite” version of Theorem
4.6
, which is also proved identically:

Theorem B.13
.

Let
\xi\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
be such that
\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\xi)<1
.

There exists a unique element

\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g)\in\mathscr{L}\otimes_{\Lambda}\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}=\mathscr{L}\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathcal{O}[[B^{\times}\backslash\widehat{B}^{\times}/U_{\infty}]]^{\mathrm{ord}}

such that its image under the natural morphism

\mathscr{L}\otimes_{\Lambda}\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}[S_{U_{n}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}\longrightarrow L[S_{U_{n}}]^{\mathrm{ord}}
coincides with
\Theta_{n,\xi}(f,g)
.

Let us put
\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g,\textup{\bf h}):=\textup{\bf h}^{B,\dagger}\left(\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g)\right)\in\mathscr{L}\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathbf{I}
. Then,
\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{ord}_{p}(\xi)}
.

The element
\Theta_{\infty,\xi}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})
is the trito-non-ordinary version of Hsieh’s balanced
p
-adic
L
-function. Thanks to Hsieh’s calculations of trilinear periods (see
[
28
, Propositions 4.9 & 4.10, Corollary 4.13]
), it enjoys identical interpolative properties. Note that we allow variation of only the third factor; as a result, the said interpolation property concerns only specialisations of weight
2
.

B.4.4.

We are now ready to introduce signed balanced
p
-adic
L
-functions, via the following “definite” analogue of Theorem
4.11
(which is proved in an identical manner):

Theorem B.14
.

There exist unique
\Theta_{\infty}^{\sharp}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})
,
\Theta_{\infty}^{\flat}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\in\mathbf{I}
such that

\begin{bmatrix}\Theta_{\infty,\alpha}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\\
\Theta_{\infty,\beta}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\end{bmatrix}=Q_{g}^{-1}M_{\log,g}\begin{bmatrix}\Theta_{\infty}^{\sharp}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\\
\Theta_{\infty}^{\flat}(f,g,\textup{\bf h})\end{bmatrix}.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
B.12
and
[
7
, Theorem 3.4]
.
∎
